He left the cabin hurriedly and soon afterward Lingard came down, and sat again facing Carter across the table. His face was grave but resolute.

“We shall get the breeze directly,” he said.

===Chinese===

他上了甲板。肖凑到桌边，自酌起来，摆弄那些酒瓶时一声不响、小心翼翼得过了头，仿佛是从易碎的瓶里量出一剂致命的毒药。卡特两手插兜，仰靠着，用冷冷的眼光把肖从头到脚打量了一遍。双桅船的大副把酒杯举到唇边，目光从杯沿上狠狠射着这位生客，慢慢喝干了杯中物。

“你黑地里找船的本事倒不小，先生。”他一字一顿地说，把杯子极其轻地放回桌上。

“嗯？什么？我太阳一落就望见你们了。”

“你还知道往哪儿看呢。”肖狠狠地盯着他说。

“我是朝西边看的，那边还有点亮光，换个明白人也都会这么做。”对方有点不耐烦地回敬。“你想把活说到哪儿去？”

“你还挺会吹自己——是不是？”

“我这辈子没见过这样的人。”卡特又摆出他那漫不经心的神气。“您像是有什么心事。”

“我不喜欢小艇不知从哪个犄角旮旯里，趁我值更悄悄溜到船边来。要说望哨，跑本国航线的谁我也不差，可我最恨让人用裹了布的桨这种不 gentleman 的把戏给绕进去。游艇军官——哼。这片海里这种游艇人肯定多得很。我认为你对我玩了一手阴的。我天黑前报我们老舵说视野里什么都没有——也确实没有。我认为你们是瞎撞撞上我们的——尽管你吹什么日落啊方位啊。胡址！我知道你们是懵着头撞到我们头上的，还用裹了的桨。这也叫体面？”

“我要真裹了桨，那是有正当理由的。我想悄悄溜过一处停着本地船的小湾。在那样一条小船上，又不带武装——像我这样——那是起码的小心。我看你们看得清清楚楚，可我绝不想吓着谁。信我一句。”

“我巴不得你们去了别处。”肖咆哮道。“我最恨因为意外和谎话被人抓住错——就这么着！我们老舵叫我了——”

他忽匆离开餐舱，随后林加德下来了，隔着桌子在卡特对面重新坐下。他脸色凝重而坚决。

“马上就要来风了。”他说。

===Original===

“Then, sir,” said Carter, getting up, “if you will give me back that letter I shall go on cruising about here to speak some other ship. I trust you will report us wherever you are going.”

“I am going to the yacht and I shall keep the letter,” answered Lingard with decision. “I know exactly where she is, and I must go to the rescue of those people. It’s most fortunate you’ve fallen in with me, Mr. Carter. Fortunate for them and fortunate for me,” he added in a lower tone.

“Yes,” drawled Carter, reflectively. “There may be a tidy bit of salvage money if you should get the vessel off, but I don’t think you can do much. I had better stay out here and try to speak some gunboat﻿—”

“You must come back to your ship with me,” said Lingard, authoritatively. “Never mind the gunboats.”

“That wouldn’t be carrying out my orders,” argued Carter. “I’ve got to speak a homeward-bound ship or a man-of-war﻿—that’s plain enough. I am not anxious to knock about for days in an open boat, but﻿—let me fill my freshwater breaker, Captain, and I will be off.”

“Nonsense,” said Lingard, sharply. “You’ve got to come with me to show the place and﻿—and help. I’ll take your boat in tow.”

Carter did not seem convinced. Lingard laid a heavy hand on his shoulder.

“Look here, young fellow. I am Tom Lingard and there’s not a white man among these islands, and very few natives, that have not heard of me. My luck brought you into my ship﻿—and now I’ve got you, you must stay. You must!”

The last “must” burst out loud and sharp like a pistol-shot. Carter stepped back.

“Do you mean you would keep me by force?” he asked, startled.

“Force,” repeated Lingard. “It rests with you. I cannot let you speak any vessel. Your yacht has gone ashore in a most inconvenient place﻿—for me; and with your boats sent off here and there, you would bring every infernal gunboat buzzing to a spot that was as quiet and retired as the heart of man could wish. You stranding just on that spot of the whole coast was my bad luck. And that I could not help. You coming upon me like this is my good luck. And that I hold!”

He dropped his clenched fist, big and muscular, in the light of the lamp on the black cloth, amongst the glitter of glasses, with the strong fingers closed tight upon the firm flesh of the palm. He left it there for a moment as if showing Carter that luck he was going to hold. And he went on:

===Chinese===

“那么，先生，”卡特起身说，“您把那封信还我，我就继续在这一带转，去搭别的一条船。想来您无论去哪儿都会替我们报个信的。”

“我要去你们那条游艇那儿，信我留下。”林加德决然回答。“我确切知道她在哪儿，我必须去救那些人。您碰上我，真是再幸运不过了，卡特先生。对他们幸运，对我也幸运。”他声音低了些，补了一句。

“是啊。”卡特慢吞吞地说，若有所思。“您要是把船弄脱了，没准有一笔可观的救助费，可我看您帮不上什么忙。我最好还是在外头转，设法搭一条炮舰——”

“你必须跟我回你们的船去。”林加德威严地说。“别管什么炮舰。”

“那可就没执行我的命令了。”卡特争辩道。“我得搭上一条回航船或者一条军舰——这明摆着的。我可不想在一条做篷艇里再荡好几天，可是——给我的淡水桶灌满水，船长，我就走。”

“胡说。”林加德厉声道。“你得跟我去带路，还有——帮忙。我会拖带你的小艇。”

卡特似乎没被说服。林加德把一只沉重的手按在他肩上。

“听我说，年轻人。我是汤姆·林加德，这些岛子里没有一个白人、也很少有本地人没听过我的名号。是我的运气把你们带进我的船——如今我逮住了你，你就得留下。你必须！”

最后那个“必须”迸出来，响亮而尖锐，像一声枪响。卡特退了一步。

“您是要说，您要用强留我？”他愕然问。

“用强。”林加德重复道。“这就看你自己了。我不能让你去搭任何船。你们那条游艇搁浅的地方，对我——麻烦透了；你们的艇一条往这儿一条往那儿，会把每一条该死的炮舰都召到那个地点嗡嗡乱转，而那地方本来静秘得人心所能指望。你们偏偏在那整条海岸的那一处上滩，是我的背运。这个我没办法。你们这样撞上我，是我的好运。这个我攥住了！”

他握紧的拳头，又大又筋肉结实，落在灯光下黑桌布上、落在杯盏的闪光之间，有力的手指紧紧扣着掌心的肌肉。他把拳头在那儿放了一会儿，仿佛给卡特看一眼他打算攥住的那个运气。然后接着说：

===Original===

“Do you know into what hornet’s nest your stupid people have blundered? How much d’ye think their lives are worth, just now? Not a brass farthing if the breeze fails me for another twenty-four hours. You may well open your eyes. It is so! And it may be too late now, while I am arguing with you here.”

He tapped the table with his knuckles, and the glasses, waking up, jingled a thin, plaintive finale to his speech. Carter stood leaning against the sideboard. He was amazed by the unexpected turn of the conversation; his jaw dropped slightly and his eyes never swerved for a moment from Lingard’s face. The silence in the cabin lasted only a few seconds, but to Carter, who waited breathlessly, it seemed very long. And all at once he heard in it, for the first time, the cabin clock tick distinctly, in pulsating beats, as though a little heart of metal behind the dial had been started into sudden palpitation.

“A gunboat!” shouted Lingard, suddenly, as if he had seen only in that moment, by the light of some vivid flash of thought, all the difficulties of the situation. “If you don’t go back with me there will be nothing left for you to go back to﻿—very soon. Your gunboat won’t find a single ship’s rib or a single corpse left for a landmark. That she won’t. It isn’t a gunboat skipper you want. I am the man you want. You don’t know your luck when you see it, but I know mine, I do﻿—and﻿—look here﻿—”

He touched Carter’s chest with his forefinger, and said with a sudden gentleness of tone:

“I am a white man inside and out; I won’t let inoffensive people﻿—and a woman, too﻿—come to harm if I can help it. And if I can’t help, nobody can. You understand﻿—nobody! There’s no time for it. But I am like any other man that is worth his salt: I won’t let the end of an undertaking go by the board while there is a chance to hold on﻿—and it’s like this﻿—”

His voice was persuasive﻿—almost caressing; he had hold now of a coat button and tugged at it slightly as he went on in a confidential manner:

“As it turns out, Mr. Carter, I would﻿—in a manner of speaking﻿—I would as soon shoot you where you stand as let you go to raise an alarm all over this sea about your confounded yacht. I have other lives to consider﻿—and friends﻿—and promises﻿—and﻿—and myself, too. I shall keep you,” he concluded, sharply.

===Chinese===

“你知道你们那些蠢货闯进的是什么样的马蜂窝？想想看，他们的命这会儿值多少？要是这风再耽搁我二十四小时，一个铜子儿都不值。你眼睛瞪那么大也没用。就是这样！说不定现在已经晚了，我还在这儿跟你磨嘴皮子。”

他用指节敲敲桌子，杯盏惊醒过来，为他这番话叮叮哓哓地敲了一串细而哀婉的尾音。卡特倚着餐具柜站着。谈话这意想不到的转折让他惊愕；他的下巴微微张着，眼睛却一刻也没有离开林加德的脸。舱内的寂静只持续了几秒钟，可对屏息等待的卡特来说，显得很长很长。忽然间，他头一回在这寂静里清清楚楚地听见了舱钟的滴嗒，一声一声搏动，仿佛表盘后面那颗小小的金属心脏被惊得突然悸动起来。

“炮舰！”林加德忽然大喊，仿佛直到这一刻，借着思想中某道炽亮的闪光，他才看清了局面的全部难处。“你不跟我回去，很快就什么都剩不下——你们想回也没得回。你们的炮舰到时候连一根船肋、一具尸首也找不着当岸标。真找不着。你们要的不是炮舰舰长。你们要的人就是我。你的运气到了眼前你都不认得，可我认得我的运气，我认得——还有——听我说——”

他用食指点着卡特的胸口，声调忽然一变，变得近乎温柔：

“我是个里里外外彻头彻尾的白人；只要我有办法，我就不会让无辜的人——还有一位太太——受到伤害。要是我没办法，谁都没办法。你明白——谁都没有！时间来不及了。可我也跟任何有血性的男人一样：只要还有一线机会抓住，我就不会让一桩事业半途而废——事情就是这样——”

他的声音很有说服力——几乎是抚慰的；他这会儿攥住了对方外衣上的一粒纽扣，轻轻扯着，用推心置腹的口气继续说：

“事情弄到这一步，卡特先生，我——这么说吧——我宁可就地一枪崩了你，也不能放你去这片海上到处为您那条该死的游艇报信。我还有别人的命要考虑——还有朋友——还有承诺——还有——还有我自己。我把你留下了。”他斩钉截铁地收尾。

===Original===

Carter drew a long breath. On the deck above, the two men could hear soft footfalls, short murmurs, indistinct words spoken near the skylight. Shaw’s voice rang out loudly in growling tones:

“Furl the royals, you tindal!”

“It’s the queerest old go,” muttered Carter, looking down on to the floor. “You are a strange man. I suppose I must believe what you say﻿—unless you and that fat mate of yours are a couple of escaped lunatics that got hold of a brig by some means. Why, that chap up there wanted to pick a quarrel with me for coming aboard, and now you threaten to shoot me rather than let me go. Not that I care much about that; for some time or other you would get hanged for it; and you don’t look like a man that will end that way. If what you say is only half true, I ought to get back to the yacht as quick as ever I can. It strikes me that your coming to them will be only a small mercy, anyhow﻿—and I may be of some use﻿—But this is the queerest.﻿ ﻿… May I go in my boat?”

“As you like,” said Lingard. “There’s a rain squall coming.”

“I am in charge and will get wet along of my chaps. Give us a good long line, Captain.”

“It’s done already,” said Lingard. “You seem a sensible sailorman and can see that it would be useless to try and give me the slip.”

“For a man so ready to shoot, you seem very trustful,” drawled Carter. “If I cut adrift in a squall, I stand a pretty fair chance not to see you again.”

“You just try,” said Lingard, drily. “I have eyes in this brig, young man, that will see your boat when you couldn’t see the ship. You are of the kind I like, but if you monkey with me I will find you﻿—and when I find you I will run you down as surely as I stand here.”

Carter slapped his thigh and his eyes twinkled.

“By the Lord Harry!” he cried. “If it wasn’t for the men with me, I would try for sport. You are so cocksure about the lot you can do, Captain. You would aggravate a saint into open mutiny.”

His easy good humour had returned; but after a short burst of laughter, he became serious.

“Never fear,” he said, “I won’t slip away. If there is to be any throat-cutting﻿—as you seem to hint﻿—mine will be there, too, I promise you, and.﻿ ﻿…”

He stretched his arms out, glanced at them, shook them a little.

“And this pair of arms to take care of it,” he added, in his old, careless drawl.

===Chinese===

卡特深深吸了一口气。头顶的甲板上，两人听得见轻轻的脚步声、短促的低语、天窗近旁含混的话音。肖的嗓音响亮地咆哮着传来：

“收顶帆，你个副水手长！”

“这真是天下最蹊跷的营生。”卡特低头瞧着地板嘟哝道。“您是个怪人。我想我只能信您的话——除非您和您那位胖大副是两个逃出来的疯子，不知怎么弄到了一条双桅船。喏，上头那位老兄差点为我上船跟人干架，您呢，宁可崩了我也不放我走。这我倒不大在乎；早晚您会为此被送上绞架；可您看着又不像那样收场的人。您的话要是有一半是真的，我就该越快回游艇越好。我看您去对他们也不过是杯水车薪，怎么着——兴许我还能搭把手——可这事实在蹊跷。……我可以坐自己的艇走吗？”

“随你的便。”林加德说。“要来雨飑了。”

“我管着艇，跟弟兄们一起淋。给我们一根够长的缆，船长。”

“已经安上了。”林加德说。“你像是个明白的水手，该看得出来想甩掉我是白搭。”

“对一个动辄要崩人的人来说，您倒挺信得过人。”卡特懒洋洋地说。“要是我在飑里砍断缆，八成就再见不着您了。”

“你只管试试。”林加德干巴巴地说。“这条船上我有的是眼睛，年轻人，你看不见船的时候它们还看得见你的艇。你这种人我喜欢，可你要是跟我耍花招，我会找到你——找到你就撞沉你，就像我站在这儿一样确定。”

卡特一拍大腿，眼睛亮了。

“老天爷作证！”他喊道。“要不是艇里还有弟兄，我真想试试玩玩。您对您那两下子也太有把提了，船长。您能把圣人激成公开哗变。”

他那随和的好脾气又回来了；可短促地笑了一阵之后，他郑重起来。

“放心，”他说，“我不会溜。真要割喉咙的话——您话里像是这个意思——我保证我那份也少不了，而且……”

他伸开双臂，看了看，又轻轻抖了抖。

“还有这双胳膊照应着呢。”他用那副老样子的、满不在乎的慢腔调补了一句。

===Original===

But the master of the brig sitting with both his elbows on the table, his face in his hands, had fallen unexpectedly into a meditation so concentrated and so profound that he seemed neither to hear, see, nor breathe. The sight of that man’s complete absorption in thought was to Carter almost more surprising than any other occurrence of that night. Had his strange host vanished suddenly from before his eyes, it could not have made him feel more uncomfortably alone in that cabin where the pertinacious clock kept ticking off the useless minutes of the calm before it would, with the same steady beat, begin to measure the aimless disturbance of the storm.

===Chinese===

可双桅船的船长却双肘支在桌上，脸埋在手里，已出人意料地坠入了一片如此专注、如此深沉的冥想，仿佛不闻、不见、也不呼吸了。目睹这个人在思绪中这般彻底的沉酣，对卡特来说，几乎比这一夜的任何一桩遭遇都更令他惊愕。哪怕他这位古怪的东道主忽然从他眼前消散，也不见得能比这更让他在这舱里感到孤立无援的不自在——舱里那只锲而不舍的钟还在滴嗒滴嗒地数着风平浪静的无用分秒，随后，它又将用同样平稳的节拍，开始计量风暴里那场漫无目的的骚动。

### III / 三
===Original===

After waiting a moment, Carter went on deck. The sky, the sea, the brig itself had disappeared in a darkness that had become impenetrable, palpable, and stifling. An immense cloud had come up running over the heavens, as if looking for the little craft, and now hung over it, arrested. To the south there was a livid trembling gleam, faint and sad, like a vanishing memory of destroyed starlight. To the north, as if to prove the impossible, an incredibly blacker patch outlined on the tremendous blackness of the sky the heart of the coming squall. The glimmers in the water had gone out and the invisible sea all around lay mute and still as if it had died suddenly of fright.

Carter could see nothing. He felt about him people moving; he heard them in the darkness whispering faintly as if they had been exchanging secrets important or infamous. The night effaced even words, and its mystery had captured everything and every sound﻿—had left nothing free but the unexpected that seemed to hover about one, ready to stretch out its stealthy hand in a touch sudden, familiar, and appalling. Even the careless disposition of the young ex-officer of an opium-clipper was affected by the ominous aspect of the hour. What was this vessel? What were those people? What would happen tomorrow? To the yacht? To himself? He felt suddenly without any additional reason but the darkness that it was a poor show, anyhow, a dashed poor show for all hands. The irrational conviction made him falter for a second where he stood and he gripped the slide of the companionway hard.

Shaw’s voice right close to his ear relieved and cleared his troubled thoughts.

“Oh! it’s you, Mister. Come up at last,” said the mate of the brig slowly. “It appears we’ve got to give you a tow now. Of all the rum incidents, this beats all. A boat sneaks up from nowhere and turns out to be a long-expected friend! For you are one of them friends the skipper was going to meet somewhere here. Ain’t you now? Come! I know more than you may think. Are we off to﻿—you may just as well tell﻿—off to﻿—h’m ha﻿ ﻿… you know?”

“Yes. I know. Don’t you?” articulated Carter, innocently.

Shaw remained very quiet for a minute.

“Where’s my skipper?” he asked at last.

“I left him down below in a kind of trance. Where’s my boat?”

===Chinese===

等了一会儿，卡特上了甲板。天、海，还有双桅船本身，都消失在一种变得无法穿透、可以触摸、令人窒息的黑暗里。一大团云在天上奔过来，仿佛在找这条小船，此刻已停住，悬在它的头顶。南边有一道惨白颤抖的微光，黯淡而愁惨，像被毁灭的星光正在消逝的记忆。北边，仿佛要把不可能的事证明给人看似的，一块难以置信地更黑的斑块，在天空巨大的黑暗上勾出了那场将至的飑的心脏。水里的点点微光都熄了，四周看不见的海默默静静地躺着，仿佛突然被吓死了一般。

卡特什么也看不见。他感觉到身边有人在走动；他听见他们在黑暗中窃窃低语，仿佛在交换什么重大的或见不得人的秘密。夜甚至把话语也抹去了，它的神秘握住了一切和每一种声音——什么也没有留下，只有那不期之事仿佛还在人四周盘旋，随时准备伸出它隐秘的手，猝然一碰，那触碰熟稔而骇人。就连这位前鸦片飞剪船军官那满不在乎的性情，也被这一时刻的不祥气象攝住了。这是条什么船？这些人是谁？明天会发生什么？游艇会怎样？他自己会怎样？没有任何新的缘由，单单是这黑暗，他忽然觉得，这一切不管怎样都是一场穷戏，对所有的人来说都是一场糟糕透顶的穷戏。这无端的确信让他在原地打了一个趔趄，他紧紧抓住了升降口滑板。

肖的嗓音恰好在他耳边响起，为他排解和澄清了纷乱的思绪。

“哦！是您哪，先生。总算上来了。”双桅船的大副慢吞吞地说。“看来咱们还得拖您一程。天下的怪事多了，这桩最怪。一条小艇不知从哪儿溜出来，一打听竟是久盼的老朋友！您就是船长要在这儿什么地儿碰的那些朋友里的一个，对不对？喏！我知道的比您想的多。咱们这是要去——您不妨说了吧——去——嗯哼……您懂的？”

“是。我懂。您不懂吗？”卡特一字一顿，天真地说。

肖沉獨了好一会儿。

“我们船长呢？”他终于问。

“我把他留在下面了，跟丢了魂儿似的。我的艇呢？”

===Original===

“Your boat is hanging astern. And my opinion is that you are as uncivil as I’ve proved you to be untruthful. Egzz-actly.”

Carter stumbled toward the taffrail and in the first step he made came full against somebody who glided away. It seemed to him that such a night brings men to a lower level. He thought that he might have been knocked on the head by anybody strong enough to lift a crowbar. He felt strangely irritated. He said loudly, aiming his words at Shaw whom he supposed somewhere near:

“And my opinion is that you and your skipper will come to a sudden bad end before﻿—”

“I thought you were in your boat. Have you changed your mind?” asked Lingard in his deep voice close to Carter’s elbow.

Carter felt his way along the rail, till his hand found a line that seemed, in the calm, to stream out of its own accord into the darkness. He hailed his boat, and directly heard the wash of water against her bows as she was hauled quickly under the counter. Then he loomed up shapeless on the rail, and the next moment disappeared as if he had fallen out of the universe. Lingard heard him say:

“Catch hold of my leg, John.” There were hollow sounds in the boat; a voice growled, “All right.”

“Keep clear of the counter,” said Lingard, speaking in quiet warning tones into the night. “The brig may get a lot of sternway on her should this squall not strike her fairly.”

“Aye, aye. I will mind,” was the muttered answer from the water.

Lingard crossed over to the port side, and looked steadily at the sooty mass of approaching vapours. After a moment he said curtly, “Brace up for the port tack, Mr. Shaw,” and remained silent, with his face to the sea. A sound, sorrowful and startling like the sigh of some immense creature, travelling across the starless space, passed above the vertical and lofty spars of the motionless brig.

===Chinese===

“您的艇吊在船尾呢。我的意见是，您跟不讲信用一样地没礼貌。一点——没错。”

卡特跌跌撞撞往船尾栏杆（taffrail）走去，头一步就撞在一个滑开的人影身上。他觉得这样的黑夜会把人拉到更低的层次上。他想，随便哪个抡得动撬棍的人都能把他敲了闷棍。他感到一种说不出的恼火。他大声说，话是冲着他料想就在近旁的肖去的：

“我的意见是，您和您那位船长早晚会落个突然的坏下场，就在——”

“我还当您回艇里去了呢。改主意了？”林加德低沉的嗓音在卡特的肘边响起。

卡特摸索着沿栏杆走，直到手碰到一根缆，在无风中，那缆仿佛自己流进黑暗里去。他呼唤他的艇，立刻听见艇被迅速拽到艉椅之下时船头扒水的哗哗声。随后他在栏杆上显出一个不成形状的黑影，下一刻便消失了，仿佛从宇宙里跌了出去。林加德听见他说：

“抓住我的腿，约翰。”艇里传来空籠般的声响；一个嗓音咕哝道：“好了。”

“离艉椅远点。”林加德对着夜色用安静的警告语气说。“这场飑要是打得不正，双桅船可能会大倒退。”

“是，是。我留意。”水上这样低声回答。

林加德跨到左舷，凝视着那团炭黑般逼近的水汽。片刻后他简短地说：“迎风转向，转左舷抢风行驶，肖先生。”说完便沉默了，脸朝着海。一种悲哀而骇人的声响，像什么庞然大物的叹息，掠过无星的天空，从静止的双桅船那笔直高耸的桅杆上方经过。

===Original===

It grew louder, then suddenly ceased for a moment, and the taut rigging of the brig was heard vibrating its answer in a singing note to this threatening murmur of the winds. A long and slow undulation lifted the level of the waters, as if the sea had drawn a deep breath of anxious suspense. The next minute an immense disturbance leaped out of the darkness upon the sea, kindling upon it a livid clearness of foam, and the first gust of the squall boarded the brig in a stinging flick of rain and spray. As if overwhelmed by the suddenness of the fierce onset, the vessel remained for a second upright where she floated, shaking with tremendous jerks from trucks to keel; while high up in the night the invisible canvas was heard rattling and beating about violently.

Then, with a quick double report, as of heavy guns, both topsails filled at once and the brig fell over swiftly on her side. Shaw was thrown headlong against the skylight, and Lingard, who had encircled the weather rail with his arm, felt the vessel under his feet dart forward smoothly, and the deck become less slanting﻿—the speed of the brig running off a little now, easing the overturning strain of the wind upon the distended surfaces of the sails. It was only the fineness of the little vessel’s lines and the perfect shape of her hull that saved the canvas, and perhaps the spars, by enabling the ready craft to get way upon herself with such lightning-like rapidity. Lingard drew a long breath and yelled jubilantly at Shaw who was struggling up against wind and rain to his commander’s side.

“She’ll do. Hold on everything.”

===Chinese===

那声响越来越近，忽然停了一瞬，只听见双桅船绷紧的索具嗡嗡地颤着，以一声歌唱般的音调，回答风的这番恫吓低语。一道缓慢的长浪抬起了平面的水位，仿佛大海焦虑地悬着，深吸了一口气。下一分钟，一团巨大的骚动从黑暗里跳到海上，在海上燃起一片惨白的泡沫亮光，飑的第一阵风夹着刺人的雨点和浪沫扑上双桅船。仿佛被这凶猛突袭的势头压倒，船在她漂浮的地方直立了一秒钟，从桅顶到龙骨剧烈地颤抖；而在高处的夜空里，听得见看不见的帆布在噼里啪啦地猛烈拍打。

接着，像两门重炮齐发般砰然两响，两张顶帆同时张满，双桅船訇然向一侧倾倒。肖被掀得天翻地转撞在天窗上，而抱着上风舷栏杆的林加德，觉得脚下的船平滑地向前猛窜，甲板的倾斜度小了些——双桅船的速度略略卸去风力压在绷满帆面上的倾覆之力。全靠这条小船线型的精致和船体的完美形状，让这条灵敏的船得以闪电般迅速地获得动力，才保住了帆，也许还有桅杆。林加德长长吸了一口气，向在风雨里挣扎着往他身边爬的肖欢呼地大喊：

“她挺住了。各处都抓稳。”

===Original===

Shaw tried to speak. He swallowed great mouthfuls of tepid water which the wind drove down his throat. The brig seemed to sail through undulating waves that passed swishing between the masts and swept over the decks with the fierce rush and noise of a cataract. From every spar and every rope a ragged sheet of water streamed flicking to leeward. The overpowering deluge seemed to last for an age; became unbearable﻿—and, all at once, stopped. In a couple of minutes the shower had run its length over the brig and now could be seen like a straight grey wall, going away into the night under the fierce whispering of dissolving clouds. The wind eased. To the northward, low down in the darkness, three stars appeared in a row, leaping in and out between the crests of waves like the distant heads of swimmers in a running surf; and the retreating edge of the cloud, perfectly straight from east to west, slipped along the dome of the sky like an immense hemispheric, iron shutter pivoting down smoothly as if operated by some mighty engine. An inspiring and penetrating freshness flowed together with the shimmer of light, through the augmented glory of the heaven, a glory exalted, undimmed, and strangely startling as if a new world had been created during the short flight of the stormy cloud. It was a return to life, a return to space; the earth coming out from under a pall to take its place in the renewed and immense scintillation of the universe.

The brig, her yards slightly checked in, ran with an easy motion under the topsails, jib and driver, pushing contemptuously aside the turbulent crowd of noisy and agitated waves. As the craft went swiftly ahead she unrolled behind her over the uneasy darkness of the sea a broad ribbon of seething foam shot with wispy gleams of dark discs escaping from under the rudder. Far away astern, at the end of a line no thicker than a black thread, which dipped now and then its long curve in the bursting froth, a toy-like object could be made out, elongated and dark, racing after the brig over the snowy whiteness of her wake.

===Chinese===

肖想说话。他大口大口地吞着风灌进他喉咙里的温水。双桅船仿佛航行在起伏的浪涛之间，浪从桅杆间喷瑝穿过，带着瀑布般凶猛的冲势和喧响扫过甲板。每一根桅桁、每一根缆绳都拖着一条破碎的水片，向背风处甩动。这势不可当的大水仿佛持续了一个世纪；变得难以忍受——忽然，停了。几分钟内，阵雨在双桅船上跑完了它的全程，此刻可以看到它像一堵笔直的灰墙，在云块消散时激烈的低语声中，退进夜里。风缓了下来。北边，低低地悬在黑暗里，三颗星排成一行，像远处激浪里游泳者的脑袋，在浪峰之间时隐时现；云块退却的边缘，从东到西笔直一条，沿着天穹滑动，像一幅巨大的半球形铁百叶，由某台强大的机器操纵着，平滑地转落下去。一股令人振奋、沁入肺腑的清新，与闪烁的微光一同流来，穿过更加辉煌的天宇——那辉煌高掦、明澈，而且出奇地惊人，仿佛在这团暴云短短的飞行期间，一个新的世界已被创造出来。这是向生命的回归，向空间的回归；大地从一幅裹尸布下走了出来，在宇宙重新燃起的浩大闪烁中各就其位。

双桅船微收着横桁，顶着两张顶帆、前桅帆和纵帆，平稳地行驶，把那群喧器激愤的浪涛轻蔑地拨到一旁。船儿疾速前行，在身后那片不安的黑暗海面上，展开一条宽阔的沸腾泡沫带，其间闪过一缕缕从舵下逃出的暗色圆盘的微光。远远的船尾，在一根不比黑线粗多少的长缆尽头——那缆的长弧不时浸进炸开的白沫里——可以辨认出一个玩具似的物体，长条形，黑黢黢的，正踏着双桅船航迹的雪白，在后面追赶。

===Original===

Lingard walked aft, and, with both his hands on the taffrail, looked eagerly for Carter’s boat. The first glance satisfied him that the yacht’s gig was towing easily at the end of the long scope of line, and he turned away to look ahead and to leeward with a steady gaze. It was then half an hour past midnight and Shaw, relieved by Wasub, had gone below. Before he went, he said to Lingard, “I will be off, sir, if you’re not going to make more sail yet.” “Not yet for a while,” had answered Lingard in a preoccupied manner; and Shaw departed aggrieved at such a neglect of making the best of a good breeze.

On the main deck dark-skinned men, whose clothing clung to their shivering limbs as if they had been overboard, had finished recoiling the braces, and clearing the gear. The kassab, after having hung the fore-topsail halyards in the becket, strutted into the waist toward a row of men who stood idly with their shoulders against the side of the long boat amidships. He passed along looking up close at the stolid faces. Room was made for him, and he took his place at the end.

“It was a great rain and a mighty wind, O men,” he said, dogmatically, “but no wind can ever hurt this ship. That I knew while I stood minding the sail which is under my care.”

A dull and inexpressive murmur was heard from the men. Over the high weather rail, a topping wave flung into their eyes a handful of heavy drops that stung like hail. There were low groans of indignation. A man sighed. Another emitted a spasmodic laugh through his chattering teeth. No one moved away. The little kassab wiped his face and went on in his cracked voice, to the accompaniment of the swishing sounds made by the seas that swept regularly astern along the ship’s side.

“Have you heard him shout at the wind﻿—louder than the wind? I have heard, being far forward. And before, too, in the many years I served this white man I have heard him often cry magic words that make all safe. *Ya-wa!* This is truth. Ask Wasub who is a Haji, even as I am.”

“I have seen white men’s ships with their masts broken﻿—also wrecked like our own praus,” remarked sadly a lean, lank fellow who shivered beside the kassab, hanging his head and trying to grasp his shoulder blades.

===Chinese===

林加德走向船尾，两手扶着船尾栏杆，急切地寻找卡特的艇。第一眼他就放了心：游艇的小艇正安然拖在长缆的尽头，他便转过身，稳稳地朝前方和背风处望去。这时已过午夜半小时，肖由瓦苏布接了班，已下舱去了。临走前他对林加德说：“您要是不再加帆，我就下去了，先生。”“暂时还不。”林加德心不在焉地回答；肖走了，心里很不满，嫌这样白白辜负了一阵好风。

主甲板上，深色皮肤的人们——衣服贴在颤抖的四肢上，仿佛刚从海里捞上来——已经收好转桁索、理好索具。库管员（kassab）把前顶帆升降索挂上索环后，大摇大摆走进船腰，走向一排闲靠着船中长大艇船舷的人。他一路凑近端详着一张张呆板的脸。人们为他让出位置，他在排尾站定。

“这雨下得真大，风也真猛，伙计们。”他武断地说，“可什么风也伤害不了这条船。我守着我看管的帆的时候，就知道这个。”

人群里响起一阵沉闷而无表情的低杂。一个高浪越过上风的 高栏杆，抡给他们一脸 heavy的水珠，打上去像冰雹一样疼。响起几声愤怒的低哼。有人叹气。另一个人从磕碰的牙齿缝里挤出一阵痉挛的笑。谁也没有走开。矮小的库管员抹了把脸，用他那嘶哑的嗓音，伴着海浪沿船舷规律地扫过船尾的琤琮声，继续说：

“你们听见他冲风吔嗬了吗——比风还响？我听见了，我在船头那边呢。再说，先前，在我伺候这个白人的许多年里，我也常听见他喊那些逢凶化吉的咒语。真的！这是实话。去问瓦苏布，他是个哈吉，跟我一样。”

“我也见过白人的船折了桅杆——也跟咱们自己的普拉帆船一样撞烂了。”一个瘦长条忧愁地说。他缩在库管员旁边，垂着头，两臂紧抱着肩胛。

===Original===

“True,” admitted the kassab. “They are all the children of Satan but to some more favour is shown. To obey such men on the sea or in a fight is good. I saw him who is master here fight with wild men who eat their enemies﻿—far away to the eastward﻿—and I dealt blows by his side without fear; for the charms he, no doubt, possesses protect his servants also. I am a believer and the Stoned One can not touch my forehead. Yet the reward of victory comes from the accursed. For six years have I sailed with that white man; first as one who minds the rudder, for I am a man of the sea, born in a prau, and am skilled in such work. And now, because of my great knowledge of his desires, I have the care of all things in this ship.”

Several voices muttered, “True. True.” They remained apathetic and patient, in the rush of wind, under the repeated short flights of sprays. The slight roll of the ship balanced them stiffly all together where they stood propped against the big boat. The breeze humming between the inclined masts enveloped their dark and silent figures in the unceasing resonance of its breath.

The brig’s head had been laid so as to pass a little to windward of the small islands of the Carimata group. They had been till then hidden in the night, but now both men on the lookout reported land ahead in one long cry. Lingard, standing to leeward abreast of the wheel, watched the islet first seen. When it was nearly abeam of the brig he gave his orders, and Wasub hurried off to the main deck. The helm was put down, the yards on the main came slowly square and the wet canvas of the main-topsail clung suddenly to the mast after a single heavy flap. The dazzling streak of the ship’s wake vanished. The vessel lost her way and began to dip her bows into the quick succession of the running head seas. And at every slow plunge of the craft, the song of the wind would swell louder amongst the waving spars, with a wild and mournful note.

===Chinese===

“不错。”库管员承认。“他们都是撒旦的子孙，可有的更得宠些。在海上或在械斗里听这种人的号令是好事。我见过这里的那个当家的，跟吃人敌尸的野人打仗——远在东边——我就毫无畏惧地在他身边挥拳；因为他身上无疑带着的护符，也保佑他的仆人。我是个信士，那被石击者碰不得我的额头。只是胜利的赏赐来自该受诅咒者。我跟这个白人跑了六年；起初是看舵的，因为我是海上人，生在普拉帆船里，这营生我熟。如今呢，因为我深知他的心意，这条船上的一切物件都归我照管。”

几个声音咕哝：“真的。真的。”他们依然冷淡而忍耐，在风的奔流里，在一阵阵短促的浪沫飞扫之下。船身轻微的横摇把他们这一排靠着大艇撑立的人僵僵地一齐摇掸着。风在倾斜的桅杆间嗡嗡作响，用它不息的呼吸的共鸣，裹住他们黝黑而沉默的身影。

双桅船的船头对得略略偏向卡里马塔（Carimata）群岛那些小岛的上风。那些岛此前一直藏在夜色里，这时两个望哨同声长呼前方有陆地。林加德站在舵轮旁背风处，盯着最先见到的那座小岛。待它差不多与双桅船正横时，他下了令，瓦苏布匆勿赶往主甲板。舵转向下风，主桅的横桁慢慢转正，湿透的主顶帆帆面猛地一拍，便贴住了桅杆。船尾那道炫目的航迹消失了。船失了速，船头开始一次次扎进接踵而来的迎头浪里。而这条船每缓缓一俯冲，风之歌便在摇曳的桅杆间响得更亮，带着一种粗野而哀愁的调子。

===Original===

Just as the brig’s boat had been swung out, ready for lowering, the yacht’s gig hauled up by its line appeared tossing and splashing on the lee quarter. Carter stood up in the stern sheets balancing himself cleverly to the disordered motion of his cockleshell. He hailed the brig twice to know what was the matter, not being able from below and in the darkness to make out what that confused group of men on the poop were about. He got no answer, though he could see the shape of a man standing by himself aft, and apparently watching him. He was going to repeat his hail for the third time when he heard the rattling of tackles followed by a heavy splash, a burst of voices, scrambling hollow sounds﻿—and a dark mass detaching itself from the brig’s side swept past him on the crest of a passing wave. For less than a second he could see on the shimmer of the night sky the shape of a boat, the heads of men, the blades of oars pointing upward while being got out hurriedly. Then all this sank out of sight, reappeared once more far off and hardly discernible, before vanishing for good.

“Why, they’ve lowered a boat!” exclaimed Carter, falling back in his seat. He remembered that he had seen only a few hours ago three native praus lurking amongst those very islands. For a moment he had the idea of casting off to go in chase of that boat, so as to find out.﻿ ﻿… Find out what? He gave up his idea at once. What could he do?

The conviction that the yacht, and everything belonging to her, were in some indefinite but very real danger, took afresh a strong hold of him, and the persuasion that the master of the brig was going there to help did not by any means assuage his alarm. The fact only served to complicate his uneasiness with a sense of mystery.

===Chinese===

双桅船的小艇刚甩出备降，游艇的小艇便顺着缆被拽了上来，在背风的船尾砰砰泼溅地颠簸。卡特站在艉座板上，灵巧地随着他那以壳小船乱糟糟的颠簸掌握着平衡。他朝双桅船喊了两声问怎么回事——从下面、在黑暗里，他辨不清艉楼上那群人乱作一团是在干什么。没人回答，尽管他看得见一个人独自站在船尾，显然在注视他。他正要喊第三声，忽然听见滑车的铿硠声，接着是重重一声落水，一片人声，攀爬的空籟声响——一大团黑影从双桅船船舷剥离出来，乘着一道过往的浪峰从他身边扫过。不到一秒钟，他在夜空的微光里看见一条小艇的轮廓、一颗颗人头、匆忙出桨时指向上方的桨叶。随即这一切沉出视野，在远处又隐约显现了一次，便永远消失了。

“咦，他们放艇下海了！”卡特往后一坐，失声叫道。他记起就在几个钟头前，他亲眼看见三条本地普拉帆船正藏在那些岛子里。他一度起意砍缆去追那条艇，好弄清楚……弄清楚什么？他立刻放弃了这念头。他能怎么样呢？

游艇以及与她相关的一切正处于某种说不清却非常真实的危险之中的信念，重新紧紧攫住了他，而双桅船船长此去是救援的这一点，经毫没有缓解他的惊慌。这事实只是给他的不安又掺上了一层神秘。

===Original===

The white man who spoke as if that sea was all his own, or as if people intruded upon his privacy by taking the liberty of getting wrecked on a coast where he and his friends did some queer business, seemed to him an undesirable helper. That the boat had been lowered to communicate with the praus seen and avoided by him in the evening he had no doubt. The thought had flashed on him at once. It had an ugly look. Yet the best thing to do after all was to hang on and get back to the yacht and warn them.﻿ ﻿… Warn them against whom? The man had been perfectly open with him. Warn them against what? It struck him that he hadn’t the slightest conception of what would happen, of what was even likely to happen. That strange rescuer himself was bringing the news of danger. Danger from the natives of course. And yet he was in communication with those natives. That was evident. That boat going off in the night.﻿ ﻿… Carter swore heartily to himself. His perplexity became positive bodily pain as he sat, wet, uncomfortable, and still, one hand on the tiller, thrown up and down in headlong swings of his boat. And before his eyes, towering high, the black hull of the brig also rose and fell, setting her stern down in the sea, now and again, with a tremendous and foaming splash. Not a sound from her reached Carter’s ears. She seemed an abandoned craft but for the outline of a man’s head and body still visible in a watchful attitude above the taffrail.

Carter told his bowman to haul up closer and hailed:

“Brig ahoy. Anything wrong?”

He waited, listening. The shadowy man still watched. After some time a curt “No” came back in answer.

“Are you going to keep hove-to long?” shouted Carter.

“Don’t know. Not long. Drop your boat clear of the ship. Drop clear. Do damage if you don’t.”

“Slack away, John!” said Carter in a resigned tone to the elderly seaman in the bow. “Slack away and let us ride easy to the full scope. They don’t seem very talkative on board there.”

===Chinese===

那个白人说话的口气，好像那片海整个儿是他自己的，又好像人家胆敢在他和他朋友做着某种古怪营生的海岸上失事，就是侵扰了他的私境——在卡特看来，这不是一个理想的救援者。那条艇是被放下去，同他傍晚望见又绕开的那几条普拉帆船联络的，对此他毫不怀疑。这念头当时就一闪而过。这事透着不祥。可说到底，最好的办法还是吊着跟回去，回到游艇上给他们报个信……报信提防谁？那个人对他可是毫无保留的。提防什么？他忽然发现，对于将要发生什么、甚至可能发生什么，他连最模糊的概念都没有。那位古怪的救援者本人，正是带来危险消息的人。危险当然来自土著。可他又正与那些土著有着联络。这很明显。那条消失在夜里的艇就是明证。……卡特在心里狠狠咒骂。他坐在艇里，湿漉漉的，难受，一动不动，一手握着舵柄，任凭小艇把他前前后后猛烈甩着，他的困窘变成了实实在在的肉体痛苦。在他眼前，高高耸立的，是双桅船黑色的船体，也在起起落落，不时把船尾猛地砸进海里，砸出一大团翻腾的白沫。一点声音也传不到卡特耳朵里。她像一条被遗弃的船，只有船尾栏杆上方还看得出一个人头和身影的轮廓，一副戒备的姿态。

卡特叫艄水手把缆收拢些，喊道：

“双桅船，喂。出事了吗？”

他等着，听着。那个模糊的人影还在瞭望。过了一会儿，一个简短的字“没”传了回来。

“你们要顶风停很久吗？”卡特大喊。

“不知道。不会久。把艇放得离船远点。放远点。不然要吃亏。”

“松缆，约翰！”卡特对艇艄那位上了年纪的水手无奈地说。“松缆，让咱们在全长缆上慢慢荡。船上那几位看来不太爱说话。”

===Original===

Even while he was speaking the line ran out and the regular undulations of the passing seas drove the boat away from the brig. Carter turned a little in his seat to look at the land. It loomed up dead to leeward like a lofty and irregular cone only a mile or a mile and a half distant. The noise of the surf beating upon its base was heard against the wind in measured detonations. The fatigue of many days spent in the boat asserted itself above the restlessness of Carter’s thoughts and, gradually, he lost the notion of the passing time without altogether losing the consciousness of his situation.

In the intervals of that benumbed stupor﻿—rather than sleep﻿—he was aware that the interrupted noise of the surf had grown into a continuous great rumble, swelling periodically into a loud roar; that the high islet appeared now bigger, and that a white fringe of foam was visible at its feet. Still there was no stir or movement of any kind on board the brig. He noticed that the wind was moderating and the sea going down with it, and then dozed off again for a minute. When next he opened his eyes with a start, it was just in time to see with surprise a new star soar noiselessly straight up from behind the land, take up its position in a brilliant constellation﻿—and go out suddenly. Two more followed, ascending together, and after reaching about the same elevation, expired side by side.

“Them’s rockets, sir﻿—ain’t they?” said one of the men in a muffled voice.

“Aye, rockets,” grunted Carter. “And now, what’s the next move?” he muttered to himself dismally.

He got his answer in the fierce swishing whirr of a slender ray of fire that, shooting violently upward from the sombre hull of the brig, dissolved at once into a dull red shower of falling sparks. Only one, white and brilliant, remained alone poised high overhead, and after glowing vividly for a second, exploded with a feeble report. Almost at the same time he saw the brig’s head fall off the wind, made out the yards swinging round to fill the main topsail, and heard distinctly the thud of the first wave thrown off by the advancing bows. The next minute the towline got the strain and his boat started hurriedly after the brig with a sudden jerk.

===Chinese===

他话音未落，缆就放了出去，过往海浪规律的起伏把小艇从大船身边推开。卡特在座位上微微侧身看陆地。那岛黑压压地正挡在背风处，像一座高而不规则的锥体，不过一英里或一英里半远近。拍击岛根的浪涛声，隔着风，一阵一阵均匀地传来。连日在小艇里的疲乏压过了卡特思绪的躁动，渐渐地，他失去了时间流逝的感觉，却并未完全失去对自身处境的知觉。

在那麻木的昏沉——与其说是睡着——的间隙里，他察觉到断续的涛声已汇成一片持续的大轰鸣，周期性地涨成响亮的怒号；察觉到那座高岛此刻显得大了，岛脚下看得见一道白色的泡沫镶边。双桅船上却仍无一丝动静。他注意到风在减弱，海也随着平息，随即又打了一会儿盹。待他再一惊睁开眼，正好诧异地看见一颗新的星从陆地背后无声地直冲而起，在一片灿烂的星群里就位——又忽然熄灭。接着又是两颗，一同升起，升到差不多的高度，便并肩湮灭了。

“那是火箭，先生——对吧？”艇里有人瓮声瓮气地说。

“对，火箭。”卡特哼道。“接下来又是什么名堂？”他忧愁地喃喃自语。

他随即得到了回答：一道纤细的火线凶猛地嗖嗖旋转着，从双桅船黢黑的船体上猛射入高空，霎时化作一阵暗红色的火星雨落下。只有一颗，白色而耀眼，独自悬在头顶高处，灿烂地燃了一秒钟，便一声轻响炸开了。几乎同时，他看见双桅船的船头偏离了风向，辨认出横桁回转、主顶帆张满，清清楚楚听见了前进的船头劈开的第一道浪的闷响。下一分钟，拖缆绷紧，他的艇猛地一拽，急急跟上了双桅船。

===Original===

Leaning forward, wide awake and attentive, Carter steered. His men sat one behind another with shoulders up, and arched backs, dozing, uncomfortable but patient, upon the thwarts. The care requisite to steer the boat properly in the track of the seething and disturbed water left by the brig in her rapid course prevented him from reflecting much upon the incertitude of the future and upon his own unusual situation.

Now he was only exceedingly anxious to see the yacht again, and it was with a feeling of very real satisfaction that he saw all plain sail being made on the brig. Through the remaining hours of the night he sat grasping the tiller and keeping his eyes on the shadowy and high pyramid of canvas gliding steadily ahead of his boat with a slight balancing movement from side to side.

===Chinese===

卡特俯身向前，完全醒了，全神贯注地掌着舵。他的部下一个个坐在座板上，耸着肩，弓着背，打着盹，难受却忍耐。要把稳舵、让小艇走在双桅船疾驰后留下的那道翻腾紊乱的水迹正中，这份小心占住了他，使他无暇多想前途的叵测和自己奇特的处境。

此刻他只是急切地想再见到那条游艇，而当他看见双桅船上张起满帆时，心里有一种非常实在的欣慰。在夜里剩下的几个钟头里，他一直坐着，紧握舵柄，眼睛盯着前方那座朦胧高耸的帆的金字塔——它微微左右摇晃着，在他的小艇前面平稳地滑行。

### IV / 四
===Original===

It was noon before the brig, piloted by Lingard through the deep channels between the outer coral reefs, rounded within pistol-shot a low hummock of sand which marked the end of a long stretch of stony ledges that, being mostly awash, showed a black head only, here and there amongst the hissing brown froth of the yellow sea. As the brig drew clear of the sandy patch there appeared, dead to windward and beyond a maze of broken water, sandspits, and clusters of rocks, the black hull of the yacht heeling over, high and motionless upon the great expanse of glittering shallows. Her long, naked spars were inclined slightly as if she had been sailing with a good breeze. There was to the lookers-on aboard the brig something sad and disappointing in the yacht’s aspect as she lay perfectly still in an attitude that in a seaman’s mind is associated with the idea of rapid motion.

“Here she is!” said Shaw, who, clad in a spotless white suit, came just then from forward where he had been busy with the anchors. “She is well on, sir﻿—isn’t she? Looks like a mudflat to me from here.”

“Yes. It is a mudflat,” said Lingard, slowly, raising the long glass to his eye. “Haul the mainsail up, Mr. Shaw,” he went on while he took a steady look at the yacht. “We will have to work in short tacks here.”

===Chinese===

到了中午，双桅船由林加德引航，穿过外围珊瑚礁之间深深的航道，在手枪射程之内绕过了一座低矮的沙丘，那沙丘标出一段漫长石脊的尽头——石脊大半没在水里，只在黄色海面嘶嘶作响的棕色泡沫间，东一处西一处地露出黑色的脊头。双桅船驶过那片沙洲之后，正对上风处、在一大片碎浪、沙嘴和礁石的迷阵之外，显出了游艇黑色的船体，倾斜着，高高地、一动不动地停在浩瀚闪光的浅滩上。她那修长裸露的桅桁微微倾着，仿佛正张满好风行驶。在双桅船上的观望者眼里，游艇的模样有一种悲哀和令人失望的东西：她纹丝不动地躺着，却摆出一个在海员心中与疾驰联系在一起的姿势。

“她在这儿呢！”刚从船头忙完锚活儿过来的肖说，他穿着一身雪白的衣服。“她搁得挺靠里，先生——是不是？从这儿看对我就是一片泥滩。”

“对。就是泥滩。”林加德缓缓地说，举起长单筒望远镜贴到眼上。“把主帆绞起来，肖先生。”他一边对游艇仔细观察，一边继续说。“咱们在这儿得用短抢风往返。”

===Original===

He put the glass down and moved away from the rail. For the next hour he handled his little vessel in the intricate and narrow channel with careless certitude, as if every stone, every grain of sand upon the treacherous bottom had been plainly disclosed to his sight. He handled her in the fitful and unsteady breeze with a matter-of-fact audacity that made Shaw, forward at his station, gasp in sheer alarm. When heading toward the inshore shoals the brig was never put round till the quick, loud cries of the leadsmen announced that there were no more than three feet of water under her keel; and when standing toward the steep inner edge of the long reef, where the lead was of no use, the helm would be put down only when the cutwater touched the faint line of the bordering foam. Lingard’s love for his brig was a man’s love, and was so great that it could never be appeased unless he called on her to put forth all her qualities and her power, to repay his exacting affection by a faithfulness tried to the very utmost limit of endurance. Every flutter of the sails flew down from aloft along the taut leeches, to enter his heart in a sense of acute delight; and the gentle murmur of water alongside, which, continuous and soft, showed that in all her windings his incomparable craft had never, even for an instant, ceased to carry her way, was to him more precious and inspiring than the soft whisper of tender words would have been to another man. It was in such moments that he lived intensely, in a flush of strong feeling that made him long to press his little vessel to his breast. She was his perfect world full of trustful joy.

===Chinese===

他放下望远镜，离开栏杆。接下来的一个钟头里，他在那曲折狭窄的航道里驾驱着他的小船，带着漫不经心的确信，仿佛那欺诈的水底每一块石头、每一粒沙都明明白白地摊在他眼前。他在阵阵无常的风里驾驱她，那股务实的胆气让站在船头岗位上的肖干着急得直喘。朝岸边的浅滩驶去时，不等到测深手急促响亮的报数喊出龙骨下只剩三英尺水，双桅船绝不回头转向；朝长礁陡峭的内缘驶去、测深锤已派不上用场时，也非要等船头浪切水线碰上边缘泡沫那道隐约的线，才肯把舵压下。林加德对他的双桅船的爱是一个男人的爱，而且爱得那样深，非得唤她拿出全部的品质和力量，以忍耐到极限的忠实来报偿他苛求的爱恋，这爱才能餍足。帆面每一次颤动都顺着绷紧的后帆边从高处传下来，以一种尖锐的喜悦进入他的心；而船舷边那轻柔的水声——绵连续、绵绵软软，说明他这条无双的船在所有的曲折里哪怕一瞬间也不曾失速——对他来说，比柔情的耳语对另一个男人更珍贵、更令人奋振。正是在这样的时刻，他活得最炽烈，在一股强烈的感情热潮中，他真想把他的小船搂进怀里。她是他完美的世界，充满了信赖的欢欣。

===Original===

The people on board the yacht, who watched eagerly the first sail they had seen since they had been ashore on that deserted part of the coast, soon made her out, with some disappointment, to be a small merchant brig beating up tack for tack along the inner edge of the reef﻿—probably with the intention to communicate and offer assistance. The general opinion among the seafaring portion of her crew was that little effective assistance could be expected from a vessel of that description. Only the sailing-master of the yacht remarked to the boatswain (who had the advantage of being his first cousin): “This man is well acquainted here; you can see that by the way he handles his brig. I shan’t be sorry to have somebody to stand by us. Can’t tell when we will get off this mud, George.”

A long board, sailed very close, enabled the brig to fetch the southern limit of discoloured water over the bank on which the yacht had stranded. On the very edge of the muddy patch she was put in stays for the last time. As soon as she had paid off on the other tack, sail was shortened smartly, and the brig commenced the stretch that was to bring her to her anchorage, under her topsails, lower staysails and jib. There was then less than a quarter of a mile of shallow water between her and the yacht; but while that vessel had gone ashore with her head to the eastward the brig was moving slowly in a west-northwest direction, and consequently, sailed﻿—so to speak﻿—past the whole length of the yacht. Lingard saw every soul in the schooner on deck, watching his advent in a silence which was as unbroken and perfect as that on board his own vessel.

===Chinese===

游艇上的人们，自从搁浅在这片荒凉海岸以来，一直急切地望着头一张帆影，很快就辨认出那是一条小商船双桅船，正沿着礁脉内缘一抢风接一抢风地艰难驶来——大概是想取得联系、提供援助。船上懂海的那部分船员的普遍意见是，这种船指不出什么有效的援助来。只有游艇的航海长对水手长（论优势，此人是他的嫡亲表弟）说：“这个人熟悉这儿；看他驾船的手法就知道了。能有个人在旁边照应着，我不反对。谁知道咱们哪天能下这片泥，乔治。”

一段贴风行驶的长抢风，让双桅船恰好抵达游艇搁浅的那片浅滩上变色水域的南界。就在泥滩的边缘，她最后一次迎风转向。船头一摆到另一舷，便利索地收了帆，双桅船顶着两张顶帆、下支索帆和前桅帆，开始了驶向锚地的那一程。这时她与游艇之间隔着不到四分之一英里的浅水；但那条船是船头朝东上的滩，而双桅船正朝西偏北方向缓缓移动，因此可以说，是从游艇的整个船身旁边驶过。林加德看见纵帆船上每一张脸都在甲板上，注视着他的到来，那份寂静，跟他自己船上一样完好无缺。

===Original===

A little man with a red face framed in white whiskers waved a gold-laced cap above the rail in the waist of the yacht. Lingard raised his arm in return. Further aft, under the white awnings, he could see two men and a woman. One of the men and the lady were in blue. The other man, who seemed very tall and stood with his arm entwined round an awning stanchion above his head, was clad in white. Lingard saw them plainly. They looked at the brig through binoculars, turned their faces to one another, moved their lips, seemed surprised. A large dog put his forepaws on the rail, and, lifting up his big, black head, sent out three loud and plaintive barks, then dropped down out of sight. A sudden stir and an appearance of excitement amongst all hands on board the yacht was caused by their perceiving that the boat towing astern of the stranger was their own second gig.

Arms were outstretched with pointing fingers. Someone shouted out a long sentence of which not a word could be made out; and then the brig, having reached the western limit of the bank, began to move diagonally away, increasing her distance from the yacht but bringing her stern gradually into view. The people aft, Lingard noticed, left their places and walked over to the taffrail so as to keep him longer in sight.

When about a mile off the bank and nearly in line with the stern of the yacht the brig’s topsails fluttered and the yards came down slowly on the caps; the fore and aft canvas ran down; and for some time she floated quietly with folded wings upon the transparent sheet of water, under the radiant silence of the sky. Then her anchor went to the bottom with a rumbling noise resembling the roll of distant thunder. In a moment her head tended to the last puffs of the northerly airs and the ensign at the peak stirred, unfurled itself slowly, collapsed, flew out again, and finally hung down straight and still, as if weighted with lead.

“Dead calm, sir,” said Shaw to Lingard. “Dead calm again. We got into this funny place in the nick of time, sir.”

===Chinese===

一个红脸膛的小个子男人，脸四周镶着白色络腮胡，在游艇腰部的栏杆上方挥着一顶金绦帽。林加德举臂回礼。再往后，在白色天篷底下，他看得见两个男人和一个女人。其中一个男人和那位太太穿的是蓝色。另一个男人看去非常高，一只手臂绕过头顶缠着天篷支柱，穿一身白。林加德把他们看得分明。他们用双筒望远镜望着双桅船，又转过脸彼此对视，嘴唇动著，像是吃了一惊。一条大狗把前爪搭上栏杆，仰起又大又黑的头，吠了三声，响亮而哀伤，随后又落下去不见了。游艇上全船的人忽然骚动和激动起来，因为他们发现拖在生客船尾的那条小艇，正是他们自己的二号大艇。

一条条手臂伸出来，手指点着。有人喊出一长串话，一个字也听不清；随后双桅船到达浅滩的西界，开始斜着离开，与游艇的距离越来越远，但船尾渐渐进入他们的视野。林加德注意到，艉部的人们离了座，走到船尾栏杆边，好把他多看一会儿。

离浅滩约一英里、几乎与游艇船尾成一线时，双桅船的顶帆抖动起来，横桁缓缓落到桅冠上；纵帆帆布也落了下来；有一阵子，她收拢翅膀，静静地浮在那片透明的水面上，在天空明亮的寂静之下。随后她的锚链铿鐄着沉入海底，那隆隆声像远处的雷滚。片刻间，船头转向北方气流的最后几口风，斜桁上的军舰旗抖了抖，慢慢展开，又塌下去，再飘出来，末了笔直地垂下，纹丝不动，仿佛坠着铅。

“死风了，先生。”肖对林加德说。“又死风了。咱们赶得正是时候进了这个有趣的地方，先生。”

===Original===

They stood for a while side by side, looking round upon the coast and the sea. The brig had been brought up in the middle of a broad belt of clear water. To the north rocky ledges showed in black and white lines upon the slight swell setting in from there. A small island stood out from the broken water like the square tower of some submerged building. It was about two miles distant from the brig. To the eastward the coast was low; a coast of green forests fringed with dark mangroves. There was in its sombre dullness a clearly defined opening, as if a small piece had been cut out with a sharp knife. The water in it shone like a patch of polished silver. Lingard pointed it out to Shaw.

“This is the entrance to the place where we are going,” he said.

Shaw stared, round-eyed.

“I thought you came here on account of this here yacht,” he stammered, surprised.

“Ah. The yacht,” said Lingard, musingly, keeping his eyes on the break in the coast. “The yacht﻿—” He stamped his foot suddenly. “I would give all I am worth and throw in a few days of life into the bargain if I could get her off and away before tonight.”

He calmed down, and again stood gazing at the land. A little within the entrance from behind the wall of forests an invisible fire belched out steadily the black and heavy convolutions of thick smoke, which stood out high, like a twisted and shivering pillar against the clear blue of the sky.

“We must stop that game, Mr. Shaw,” said Lingard, abruptly.

“Yes, sir. What game?” asked Shaw, looking round in wonder.

“This smoke,” said Lingard, impatiently. “It’s a signal.”

“Certainly, sir﻿—though I don’t see how we can do it. It seems far inland. A signal for what, sir?”

“It was not meant for us,” said Lingard in an unexpectedly savage tone. “Here, Shaw, make them put a blank charge into that forecastle gun. Tell ’em to ram hard the wadding and grease the mouth. We want to make a good noise. If old Jörgenson hears it, that fire will be out before you have time to turn round twice.﻿ ﻿… In a minute, Mr. Carter.”

The yacht’s boat had come alongside as soon as the brig had been brought up, and Carter had been waiting to take Lingard on board the yacht. They both walked now to the gangway. Shaw, following his commander, stood by to take his last orders.

===Chinese===

两人并肩站了一会儿，环视着海岸和海。双桅船抛锚在一条宽阔的清水带的中央。北边，石脊在从那边滚来的微涌上显出一道道黑线白线。一座小岛从碎浪中挺出，像某座沉没建筑的方塔。离双桅船约两英里。往东，海岸低平；那是一片绿色森林的海岸，镶着深色红树的边。在那阴沉黯淡之中有一道轮廓分明的豁口，仿佛有人用快刀挖掉了一小块。豁口里的水闪着光，像一块擦亮的银斑。林加德把它指给肖看。

“这就是咱们要去的地方的入口。”他说。

肖瞪圆了眼睛。

“我还当您是为了这条游艇来的呢。”他磕磕巴巴地说，很是诧异。

“啊。游艇。”林加德若有所思地说，眼睛盯着海岸上那道豁口。“游艇——”他忽然跺了跺脚。“要是能让她今晚之前脱浅走人，我肯拿出我全部家当，再饶上几天阳寿。”

他平静下来，又站着凝望陆地。入口里面不远，从森林的墙背后，一堆看不见的火稳稳地吐出又黑又重的浓烟，高高直起，像一根扭曲颤抖的柱子，映在晴朗的蓝天上。

“咱们得叫停那个把戏，肖先生。”林加德唐突地说。

“是，先生。什么把戏？”肖诧异地四下看看。

“这烟。”林加德不耐烦地说。“是信号。”

“可不是，先生——不过我看咱们怎么叫停它。它像是在很里面的内陆。给谁打的信号，先生？”

“不是打给咱们的。”林加德用一种出人意料的凶狠腔调说。“听着，肖，叫他们给前甲板那门炮装一发空包。叫他们把填塞狠狠捣实，炮口抹上油。咱们要弄出个好大的响。要是老约根森听见了，你转两转身的工夫那火就得灭。……就来，卡特先生。”

双桅船一抛锚，游艇的小艇就靠了上来，卡特一直等着接林加德上游艇。这会儿两人一起走向舷门。肖跟在船长身后，侍立听候最后的命令。

===Original===

“Put all the boats in the water, Mr. Shaw,” Lingard was saying, with one foot on the rail, ready to leave his ship, “and mount the four-pounder swivel in the longboat’s bow. Cast off the sea lashings of the guns, but don’t run ’em out yet. Keep the topsails loose and the jib ready for setting, I may want the sails in a hurry. Now, Mr. Carter, I am ready for you.”

“Shove off, boys,” said Carter as soon as they were seated in the boat. “Shove off, and give way for a last pull before you get a long rest.”

The men lay back on their oars, grunting. Their faces were drawn, grey and streaked with the dried salt sprays. They had the worried expression of men who had a long call made upon their endurance. Carter, heavy-eyed and dull, steered for the yacht’s gangway. Lingard asked as they were crossing the brig’s bows:

“Water enough alongside your craft, I suppose?”

“Yes. Eight to twelve feet,” answered Carter, hoarsely. “Say, Captain! Where’s your show of cutthroats? Why! This sea is as empty as a church on a weekday.”

The booming report, nearly over his head, of the brig’s eighteen-pounder interrupted him. A round puff of white vapour, spreading itself lazily, clung in fading shreds about the foreyard. Lingard, turning half round in the stern sheets, looked at the smoke on the shore. Carter remained silent, staring sleepily at the yacht they were approaching. Lingard kept watching the smoke so intensely that he almost forgot where he was, till Carter’s voice pronouncing sharply at his ear the words “way enough,” recalled him to himself.

They were in the shadow of the yacht and coming alongside her ladder. The master of the brig looked upward into the face of a gentleman, with long whiskers and a shaved chin, staring down at him over the side through a single eyeglass. As he put his foot on the bottom step he could see the shore smoke still ascending, unceasing and thick; but even as he looked the very base of the black pillar rose above the ragged line of treetops. The whole thing floated clear away from the earth, and rolling itself into an irregularly shaped mass, drifted out to seaward, travelling slowly over the blue heavens, like a threatening and lonely cloud.

===Chinese===

“把所有的小艇都放下水，肖先生。”林加德一只脚蹬着栏杆，准备离船，嘴里吩咐着，“把那门四磅回旋炮架到长大艇的船头。解开炮的海上捆索，先别推出来。顶帆保持松着，前桅帆随时待张，我可能急着要帆。好了，卡特先生，我这就跟你走。”

“撑开，伙计们。”两人一在艇里坐定，卡特便说。“撑开，再好好划最后一程，然后你们就能歇个长假了。”

桨手们仰身向后拉桨，嘴里哼哧着。他们的脸绷着，灰扑扑的，结着干盐沫的道道痕迹，一副耐力被长时间透支后忧心的神情。卡特眼皮沉重、神情滞涩，把船导向游艇的舷门。两人划过双桅船船头时，林加德问：

“你们船边上的水大概够深吧？”

“够。八到十二英尺。”卡特声音沙哑地回答。“哎，船长！您那帮杀手呢？在哪儿呢？嘺！这片海空得跟平日的教堂一样。”

几乎就在他头顶上，双桅船那门十八磅炮砰然一声巨响，打断了他。一团白色的圆烟散漫地铺开，残片恋恋地缠在前横桁上。林加德在艉座板上侧过半个身子，望着岸上的烟。卡特不说话，睡意惺忪地盯着越来越近的游艇。林加德全神贯注地盯着那股烟，几乎忘了自己身在何处，直到卡特在耳边干脆地喊出那声“收桨”，才把他唤回自己。

他们已进了游艇的影子，正靠向她的绳梯。双桅船的船长抬眼向上，正对上一位绅士的脸——此人留着长络腮胡，下巴剃得光光，正隔着船舷用单片眼镜俯视他。他一脚踩上最底一级踏板时，还看得见岸上的烟仍在升腾，不息而浓黑；可就在他望着的时候，那根黑柱的根部竟已升到参差树梢的线条之上。整团烟脱离了地面，滚成一个形状不规则的块，向海上漂去，缓缓地越过蓝天，像一朵带着威胁的、孤独的云。

### I / 一
===Original===

The coast off which the little brig, floating upright above her anchor, seemed to guard the high hull of the yacht has no distinctive features. It is land without form. It stretches away without cape or bluff, long and low﻿—indefinitely; and when the heavy gusts of the northeast monsoon drive the thick rain slanting over the sea, it is seen faintly under the grey sky, black and with a blurred outline like the straight edge of a dissolving shore. In the long season of unclouded days, it presents to view only a narrow band of earth that appears crushed flat upon the vast level of waters by the weight of the sky, whose immense dome rests on it in a line as fine and true as that of the sea horizon itself.

Notwithstanding its nearness to the centres of European power, this coast has been known for ages to the armed wanderers of these seas as “The Shore of Refuge.” It has no specific name on the charts, and geography manuals don’t mention it at all; but the wreckage of many defeats unerringly drifts into its creeks. Its approaches are extremely difficult for a stranger. Looked at from seaward, the innumerable islets fringing what, on account of its vast size, may be called the mainland, merge into a background that presents not a single landmark to point the way through the intricate channels. It may be said that in a belt of sea twenty miles broad along that low shore there is much more coral, mud, sand, and stones than actual sea water. It was amongst the outlying shoals of this stretch that the yacht had gone ashore and the events consequent upon her stranding took place.

The diffused light of the short daybreak showed the open water to the westward, sleeping, smooth and grey, under a faded heaven. The straight coast threw a heavy belt of gloom along the shoals, which, in the calm of expiring night, were unmarked by the slightest ripple. In the faint dawn the low clumps of bushes on the sandbanks appeared immense.

===Chinese===

小双桅横帆船在锚上直挺挺地浮着，仿佛守在那艘游艇高耸的船体旁边；它们所在的这片海岸没有鲜明的特色。这是一片没有形状的陆地。它绵延开去，没有海角，没有陡崖，又长又低——漫无际涯；东北季风挟着骤雨斜扫过海面时，它在灰色天空下只隐隐现出一线黑影，轮廓模糊，像一道正在消融的海岸的直边。在万里无云的漫长季节里，它入眼的只是一窄条土地，仿佛被天空的重量压扁在浩瀚的水平洋面上；那巨大的穹隆便落在这条土地上，其界线同海平线本身一样细密、一样笔直。

这片海岸虽离欧洲各强权的中心不远，几个世纪以来却被这些海域的武装浪客们称作「避难之岸」。海图上没有它的专名，地理手册对它只字不提；可许多次败绩的残骸，却总能准确无误地漂进它的小湾。对陌生人来说，驶近它的水路极其艰难。从海上望去，环列在那片因其广袤而堪称大陆的土地边的无数小岛，融成一片背景，找不出一个显著的陆标来指点那迷宫般水道的路径。可以说，沿着那条低平海岸宽达二十英里的一带海面上，珊瑚、淤泥、泥沙和石块远比真正的海水为多。游艇便是在这一带外围的浅滩上搁浅的，随之而来的种种事件也都发生于此。

短暂破晓的漫射微光里，西边开阔的海面沉睡着，平滑而灰暗，覆在一方褪了色的天空下。笔直的海岸沿着浅滩投下一条浓重的暗带；在渐逝的夜的静定中，浅滩上连一丝涟漪也没有。朦胧的晨光里，沙洲上低矮的灌木丛显得庞大无比。

===Original===

Two figures, noiseless like two shadows, moved slowly over the beach of a rocky islet, and stopped side by side on the very edge of the water. Behind them, between the mats from which they had arisen, a small heap of black embers smouldered quietly. They stood upright and perfectly still, but for the slight movement of their heads from right to left and back again as they swept their gaze through the grey emptiness of the waters where, about two miles distant, the hull of the yacht loomed up to seaward, black and shapeless, against the wan sky.

The two figures looked beyond without exchanging as much as a murmur. The taller of the two grounded, at arm’s length, the stock of a gun with a long barrel; the hair of the other fell down to its waist; and, near by, the leaves of creepers drooping from the summit of the steep rock stirred no more than the festooned stone. The faint light, disclosing here and there a gleam of white sandbanks and the blurred hummocks of islets scattered within the gloom of the coast, the profound silence, the vast stillness all round, accentuated the loneliness of the two human beings who, urged by a sleepless hope, had risen thus, at break of day, to look afar upon the veiled face of the sea.

“Nothing!” said the man with a sigh, and as if awakening from a long period of musing.

===Chinese===

两个人影，像两道影子一样无声无息，缓缓走过一座石质小岛的滩头，并肩在水边站定。在他们身后，两人方才起身的那几张席子之间，一小堆黑炭还在静静闷烧。他们直挺挺立着，一动不动，只有脑袋缓缓地由右向左、又由左向右转动，扫视着灰茫茫空荡荡的水面；约两英里外，游艇的船身在海那边黑黢黢地耸起，衬着惨白的天空，没有形状。

两个人影朝远处望着，连一声低语也没有交换。较高的那个把一杆长筒枪的枪托抵在地上，伸直了手臂；另一个的头发一直垂到腰际；近旁，从陡崖顶上垂下的藤蔓叶子，纹丝不动，同披挂着藤萝的石头一般。微弱的光线这里那里照出白色沙洲的一角闪光、照出散布在海岸暗影中的小岛模糊的圆丘；四下里深沉的寂静、浩大的静谧，愈发衬出这两个人影的孤单——他们被一个不眠的希望驱使着，就这样在天破晓时起身，远远眺望大海那蒙着面纱的容颜。

「什么也没有！」男人叹了口气说，像是从长长的沉思中醒来。

===Original===

He was clad in a jacket of coarse blue cotton, of the kind a poor fisherman might own, and he wore it wide open on a muscular chest the colour and smoothness of bronze. From the twist of threadbare sarong wound tightly on the hips protruded outward to the left the ivory hilt, ringed with six bands of gold, of a weapon that would not have disgraced a ruler. Silver glittered about the flintlock and the hardwood stock of his gun. The red and gold handkerchief folded round his head was of costly stuff, such as is woven by highborn women in the households of chiefs, only the gold threads were tarnished and the silk frayed in the folds. His head was thrown back, the dropped eyelids narrowed the gleam of his eyes. His face was hairless, the nose short with mobile nostrils, and the smile of careless good-humour seemed to have been permanently wrought, as if with a delicate tool, into the slight hollows about the corners of rather full lips. His upright figure had a negligent elegance. But in the careless face, in the easy gestures of the whole man there was something attentive and restrained.

After giving the offing a last searching glance, he turned and, facing the rising sun, walked barefooted on the elastic sand. The trailed butt of his gun made a deep furrow. The embers had ceased to smoulder. He looked down at them pensively for a while, then called over his shoulder to the girl who had remained behind, still scanning the sea:

“The fire is out, Immada.”

===Chinese===

他穿着一件粗蓝布短褂，是穷渔夫可能有的那种，敞开着怀，露出青铜色平滑而筋肉结实的胸膛。一条磨旧的纱笼（sarong）紧紧缠在胯上，左边斜插出一柄武器的象牙柄，柄上箍着六道金环——这样的兵器拿在君王手里也不失体面。他的燧发枪上、硬木枪托上，处处闪着白银的光。裹在头上那块红金相间的头巾是贵重料子，出自头人宅邸中高贵妇人之手，只是金线已经黯淡，丝绸在褶子里也磨出了毛边。他昂着头，微垂的眼睑把眸中的光收细了。他脸上无须，鼻子短，鼻翼灵动；一副漫不经心的好性子的笑意，仿佛是用一件精巧的工具，永久地镌在了那两片相当丰润的嘴唇角的浅窝里。他挺立的身姿带着一种疏懒的优雅。可在那副随意的脸上、在那整个人松弛的举止里，藏着一种专注与克制。

他对海面投去最后审视的一瞥，随即转过身来，面向初升的太阳，赤脚踏上富有弹性的沙地。拖在身后的枪托划出一道深沟。炭火已不再冒烟。他低头对着灰烬出了一会儿神，然后回头唤那仍留在原处继续眺望大海的姑娘：

「火灭了，伊玛达（Immada）。」

===Original===

At the sound of his voice the girl moved toward the mats. Her black hair hung like a mantle. Her sarong, the kilt-like garment which both sexes wear, had the national check of grey and red, but she had not completed her attire by the belt, scarves, the loose upper wrappings, and the head-covering of a woman. A black silk jacket, like that of a man of rank, was buttoned over her bust and fitted closely to her slender waist. The edge of a stand-up collar, stiff with gold embroidery, rubbed her cheek. She had no bracelets, no anklets, and although dressed practically in man’s clothes, had about her person no weapon of any sort. Her arms hung down in exceedingly tight sleeves slit a little way up from the wrist, gold-braided and with a row of small gold buttons. She walked, brown and alert, all of a piece, with short steps, the eyes lively in an impassive little face, the arched mouth closed firmly; and her whole person breathed in its rigid grace the fiery gravity of youth at the beginning of the task of life﻿—at the beginning of beliefs and hopes.

This was the day of Lingard’s arrival upon the coast, but, as is known, the brig, delayed by the calm, did not appear in sight of the shallows till the morning was far advanced. Disappointed in their hope to see the expected sail shining in the first rays of the rising sun, the man and the woman, without attempting to relight the fire, lounged on their sleeping mats. At their feet a common canoe, hauled out of the water, was, for more security, moored by a grass rope to the shaft of a long spear planted firmly on the white beach, and the incoming tide lapped monotonously against its stern.

The girl, twisting up her black hair, fastened it with slender wooden pins. The man, reclining at full length, had made room on his mat for the gun﻿—as one would do for a friend﻿—and, supported on his elbow, looked toward the yacht with eyes whose fixed dreaminess like a transparent veil would show the slow passage of every gloomy thought by deepening gradually into a sombre stare.

“We have seen three sunrises on this islet, and no friend came from the sea,” he said without changing his attitude, with his back toward the girl who sat on the other side of the cold embers.

===Chinese===

听到他的声音，姑娘向席子那边走去。她的黑发像一件斗篷垂着。她的纱笼——男女通穿的、酷似苏格兰短裙的那种衣着——是灰红相间的国花样纹，可她没有系腰带，没有披肩巾，没有女人出门时宽松的外披和头上罩的东西，一身装束尚未齐全。一件黑绸短褂，同贵族男子的式样一样，胸前扣着扣子，紧贴着她纤细的腰。硬挺的金绣立领的边沿蹭着她的脸颊。她没有手镯，没有脚镯；虽说几乎穿的是男装，身上却一件兵刃也没有。她的双臂垂在两只极紧的袖筒里，袖口从腕上起开着一道小衩，缀着金绦和一排小金扣。她走过来，棕色的皮肤，机警利落，浑然一体，步子碎小；一张不动声色的小脸上眼睛活泼，弓形的嘴抿得紧紧的；她整个人在那份挺拔的优雅里，透着青春临到人生事业开端时的火热与庄重——在信念与希望的开端。

这一天正是林加德抵达这片海岸的日子；但如前所述，双桅船被无风耽搁，直到上午过了大半才出现在浅滩的视界之内。指望看见期待中的船帆在初升的太阳第一缕光线里闪亮的愿望落了空，这一男一女便没有再点火，懒懒地歪在睡席上。在他们脚边，一只普通的独木舟拖出水来，为稳妥起见，用一根草绳拴在一杆长矛的杆上——矛深深插在白色的沙滩里；上涨的潮水单调地舐着船尾。

姑娘把黑发拢起来盘好，用细细的木簪别住。男人直挺挺侧卧着，在席子上给自己的枪让出一块地方——像给一位朋友让位——支着胳膊肘，朝游艇那边望着；他眼中那份凝定的迷蒙像一层透明的纱，每一种阴郁的念头经过，都会叫它渐渐加深，变成一片幽暗的瞪视。

「我们在这小岛上看过了三回日出，海上还没有朋友来。」他说，姿势未变，背朝着坐在冷灰那一侧的姑娘。

===Original===

“Yes; and the moon is waning,” she answered in a low voice. “The moon is waning. Yet he promised to be here when the nights are light and the water covers the sandbanks as far as the bushes.”

“The traveller knows the time of his setting out, but not the time of his return,” observed the man, calmly.

The girl sighed.

“The nights of waiting are long,” she murmured.

“And sometimes they are vain,” said the man with the same composure. “Perhaps he will never return.”

“Why?” exclaimed the girl.

“The road is long and the heart may grow cold,” was the answer in a quiet voice. “If he does not return it is because he has forgotten.”

“Oh, Hassim, it is because he is dead,” cried the girl, indignantly.

The man, looking fixedly to seaward, smiled at the ardour of her tone.

They were brother and sister, and though very much alike, the family resemblance was lost in the more general traits common to the whole race.

They were natives of Wajo and it is a common saying amongst the Malay race that to be a successful traveller and trader a man must have some Wajo blood in his veins. And with those people trading, which means also travelling afar, is a romantic and an honourable occupation. The trader must possess an adventurous spirit and a keen understanding; he should have the fearlessness of youth and the sagacity of age; he should be diplomatic and courageous, so as to secure the favour of the great and inspire fear in evildoers.

These qualities naturally are not expected in a shopkeeper or a Chinaman pedlar; they are considered indispensable only for a man who, of noble birth and perhaps related to the ruler of his own country, wanders over the seas in a craft of his own and with many followers; carries from island to island important news as well as merchandise; who may be trusted with secret messages and valuable goods; a man who, in short, is as ready to intrigue and fight as to buy and sell. Such is the ideal trader of Wajo.

===Chinese===

「是啊；月亮也亏了。」她低声答道，「月亮亏了。可他答应过，等夜色明亮的时节，等潮水漫过沙洲、一直淹到灌木丛边，他就到。」

「出门的人知道自己几时动身，却不知道自己几时回来。」男人平静地说。

姑娘叹了口气。

「等候的夜太长了。」她低声说。

「有时候也等得白等。」男人以同样的平静说，「也许他永远不会回来了。」

「为什么？」姑娘喊起来。

「路远，心会冷掉。」对方安静地回答，「他不回来，就是因为他忘了。」

「哦，哈西姆（Hassim），那是因为他死了。」姑娘愤然叫道。

男人凝望着海那边，为她语气里的热烈微微一笑。

他们是兄妹，长相十分相似；可那点家族的相像，淹没了全种族共有的更一般的面貌里。

他们是瓦焦人；马来人中间有句常言：一个人要做成功的行商旅客，血脉里非得有几分瓦焦的血。在他们那种人看来，经商——那同时意味着远游——是一种浪漫而体面的营生。行商须有冒险的精神和敏锐的头脑；应有青年的无畏和老成的睿智；应能折冲樽俎而又胆气过人，好博得权贵的眷顾，又叫为非作歹之徒胆寒。

这些品质，自然没人指望小店主或中国货郎具备；唯有这样一种人才被认为缺一不可：他出身高贵，或许还是本国君王的亲戚，驾着自家的船只、带着众多随从漂洋过海；岛与岛之间，既贩货物，也传要讯；人们可以把密信和财货放心托付给他——总而言之，一个搞起阴谋和厮杀来同买卖一样在行的人。这便是瓦焦人心目中理想的行商。

===Original===

Trading, thus understood, was the occupation of ambitious men who played an occult but important part in all those national risings, religious disturbances, and also in the organized piratical movements on a large scale which, during the first half of the last century, affected the fate of more than one native dynasty and, for a few years at least, seriously endangered the Dutch rule in the East. When, at the cost of much blood and gold, a comparative peace had been imposed on the islands the same occupation, though shorn of its glorious possibilities, remained attractive for the most adventurous of a restless race. The younger sons and relations of many a native ruler traversed the seas of the Archipelago, visited the innumerable and little-known islands, and the then practically unknown shores of New Guinea; every spot where European trade had not penetrated﻿—from Aru to Atjeh, from Sumbawa to Palawan.

===Chinese===

这样理解的经商，是有志男儿的事业。在上世纪上半叶，他们在所有那些举国起义、宗教骚乱中，也在大规模有组织的海盗活动中，扮演着隐秘而重要的角色；那些风潮曾左右过不止一个土著王朝的命运，至少有那么几年，还严重动摇过荷兰人在东方的统治。待到耗费了许多鲜血与黄金、强加给群岛一个相对的太平之后，这同一营生虽然被剪去了光荣的可能，对那个不安分种族中最富冒险心的人，依然有其吸引力。许多土著君王的幼弟子侄纵横群岛诸海，踏访那无数鲜为人知的岛屿，还到过当时几乎完全不为人知的新几内亚海岸；凡是欧洲贸易尚未渗透的角落，无处不至——从阿鲁到亚齐，从松巴哇到巴拉望。

### II / 二
===Original===

It was in the most unknown perhaps of such spots, a small bay on the coast of New Guinea, that young Pata Hassim, the nephew of one of the greatest chiefs of Wajo, met Lingard for the first time.

He was a trader after the Wajo manner, and in a stout seagoing prau armed with two guns and manned by young men who were related to his family by blood or dependence, had come in there to buy some birds of paradise skins for the old Sultan of Ternate; a risky expedition undertaken not in the way of business but as a matter of courtesy toward the aged Sultan who had entertained him sumptuously in that dismal brick palace at Ternate for a month or more.

While lying off the village, very much on his guard, waiting for the skins and negotiating with the treacherous coast-savages who are the go-betweens in that trade, Hassim saw one morning Lingard’s brig come to an anchor in the bay, and shortly afterward observed a white man of great stature with a beard that shone like gold, land from a boat and stroll on unarmed, though followed by four Malays of the brig’s crew, toward the native village.

Hassim was struck with wonder and amazement at the cool recklessness of such a proceeding; and, after; in true Malay fashion, discussing with his people for an hour or so the urgency of the case, he also landed, but well escorted and armed, with the intention of going to see what would happen.

The affair really was very simple, “such as”﻿—Lingard would say﻿—“such as might have happened to anybody.” He went ashore with the intention to look for some stream where he could conveniently replenish his water casks, this being really the motive which had induced him to enter the bay.

While, with his men close by and surrounded by a mop-headed, sooty crowd, he was showing a few cotton handkerchiefs, and trying to explain by signs the object of his landing, a spear, lunged from behind, grazed his neck. Probably the Papuan wanted only to ascertain whether such a creature could be killed or hurt, and most likely firmly believed that it could not; but one of Lingard’s seamen at once retaliated by striking at the experimenting savage with his parang﻿—three such choppers brought for the purpose of clearing the bush, if necessary, being all the weapons the party from the brig possessed.

===Chinese===

正是在这类地方中或许最不为人知的一处——新几内亚海岸的一个小湾里——瓦久最伟大的酋长之一的侄子、年轻的帕塔·哈西姆，第一次遇见了林加德。

他是瓦久式的商人，坐着一条结实的远航普拉帆船，船上装着两门炮，水手都是与他家有血缘或依附关系的年轻人。他到那里去，是要为德尔纳特的老苏丹采买些天堂鸟皮；这趟风险之旅不是为了生意，而是出于对那位老苏丹的礼数——老苏丹曾在德尔纳特那座阴森的砖砌宫殿里盛情款待过他一个多月。

哈西姆停在村外海面上，十分戒备，一面等鸟皮，一面跟那桩买卖中充当中介的、奸诈的海岸野人讨价还价。一天早晨，他看见林加德的双桅船进湾抛锚，不多时又见一个身材魁伟的白人下了小艇，胡子闪着金光，两手空空地在岸上溜达，身后只跟着双桅船上的四个马来水手，径直朝土人的村子走去。

哈西姆被这种举动的满不在乎的莽撞惊得目瞪口呆；他照道地的马来做派，跟自己的人把这事的紧急性议论了一个来钟头，然后也上了岸——当然护卫齐全、带着武器——打算去看看会有什么好戏。

事情本身实在简单得很，“这种事”——林加德后来说——“谁都可能碰上。”他上岸是想找一条溪流，方便补满他的水桶，这才是他进湾的真实缘由。

他让手下紧挨着自己，被一团乱发蓬松、乌黑锃亮的人围着，正展示几条棉布手帕，打着手势说明登岸的来意，一支长矛从背后櫂来，擦过他的脖颈。那巴布亚人多半只是想试试这种生灵能不能杀死或杀伤，而且八成笃信杀不死；可林加德的一名水手立刻还手，抡起帕兰刀就朝那做试验的野人劈去——为开路带上岸的三把砍刀，就是双桅船这一行人的全部武器。

===Original===

A deadly tumult ensued with such suddenness that Lingard, turning round swiftly, saw his defender, already speared in three places, fall forward at his feet. Wasub, who was there, and afterward told the story once a week on an average, used to horrify his hearers by showing how the man blinked his eyes quickly before he fell. Lingard was unarmed. To the end of his life he remained incorrigibly reckless in that respect, explaining that he was “much too quick tempered to carry firearms on the chance of a row. And if put to it,” he argued, “I can make shift to kill a man with my fist anyhow; and then﻿—don’t ye see﻿—you know what you’re doing and are not so apt to start a trouble from sheer temper or funk﻿—see?”

In this case he did his best to kill a man with a blow from the shoulder and catching up another by the middle flung him at the naked, wild crowd. “He hurled men about as the wind hurls broken boughs. He made a broad way through our enemies!” related Wasub in his jerky voice. It is more probable that Lingard’s quick movements and the amazing aspect of such a strange being caused the warriors to fall back before his rush.

Taking instant advantage of their surprise and fear, Lingard, followed by his men, dashed along the kind of ruinous jetty leading to the village which was erected as usual over the water. They darted into one of the miserable huts built of rotten mats and bits of decayed canoes, and in this shelter showing daylight through all its sides, they had time to draw breath and realize that their position was not much improved.

===Chinese===

一场要命的混战陡然爆发。林加德飞快转身，只见护他的那人身上已中了三矛，扑面倒在他脚前。瓦苏布当时在场，此后平均每周把这个故事讲一遍，讲时总要吓唬听众：那人倒下之前眼睛怎样飞快地眨了眨。林加德没有武器。他一辈子在这方面都无可救药地莽撞，还振振有词地解释说，他“火气太急，不能带着火器去惹是生非。真被逼急了，”他说，“我拿拳头也能凑合弄死一个人；而且——你们明白不明白——你手里有数，就不容易光凭火气或害怕去惹祸——懂吗？”

这一回，他尽了最大努力，一记横肩撞向一个人，又揪住另一个的腰，把他掼进那群赤裸的野人堆里。“他把人抡来掷去，像风抡断枝。他在敌人中间开出一条大道！”瓦苏布用他短促的嗓音讲述道。其实更可能的是，林加德的迅疾动作和这样一个怪物骇人的模样，叫那些武士在他的猛冲面前自己退了。

趁着对方的惊愕与恐惧，林加德带着手下冲上通往村子的那条破烂栈桥——那村子照例架在水上。他们蹿进一间用烂席子和独木舟烂片搭成的破棚子，四壁透着天光，才算得了个喘息的地方，也才明白处境并没有好多少。

===Original===

The women and children screaming had cleared out into the bush, while at the shore end of the jetty the warriors capered and yelled, preparing for a general attack. Lingard noticed with mortification that his boat-keeper apparently had lost his head, for, instead of swimming off to the ship to give the alarm, as he was perfectly able to do, the man actually struck out for a small rock a hundred yards away and was frantically trying to climb up its perpendicular side. The tide being out, to jump into the horrible mud under the houses would have been almost certain death. Nothing remained therefore﻿—since the miserable dwelling would not have withstood a vigorous kick, let alone a siege﻿—but to rush back on shore and regain possession of the boat. To this Lingard made up his mind quickly and, arming himself with a crooked stick he found under his hand, sallied forth at the head of his three men. As he bounded along, far in advance, he had just time to perceive clearly the desperate nature of the undertaking, when he heard two shots fired to his right. The solid mass of black bodies and frizzly heads in front of him wavered and broke up. They did not run away, however.

Lingard pursued his course, but now with that thrill of exultation which even a faint prospect of success inspires in a sanguine man. He heard a shout of many voices far off, then there was another report of a shot, and a musket ball fired at long range spurted a tiny jet of sand between him and his wild enemies. His next bound would have carried him into their midst had they awaited his onset, but his uplifted arm found nothing to strike. Black backs were leaping high or gliding horizontally through the grass toward the edge of the bush.

He flung his stick at the nearest pair of black shoulders and stopped short. The tall grasses swayed themselves into a rest, a chorus of yells and piercing shrieks died out in a dismal howl, and all at once the wooded shores and the blue bay seemed to fall under the spell of a luminous stillness. The change was as startling as the awakening from a dream. The sudden silence struck Lingard as amazing.

===Chinese===

尖叫着的妇女儿童早已逃进丛林，栈桥的岸端，武士们又蹦又吼，酝酿着总攻。林加德懊恼地发现，留守小艇的那人显然吓昏了头：他明明有能力游回船上报警，却偏偏朝一百码外的一块小礁石游去，发疯似的往那垂直的石壁上爬。正退潮，跳进屋子底下那片可怕的烂泥里几乎必死无疑。那间破棚子经不起一脚猛踹，更别说围攻了——因此别无他法，只有杀回岸去，夺回小艇。林加德当机立断，随手操起一根弯棍，领着剩下的三个人冲了出去。他一路飞跃在前，刚来得及看清这举动有多绝望，右前方就响了两枪。他面前那一大团黑色的身躯和卷毛脑袋晃了晃，散开了。但他们并没有逃。

林加德继续前进，此刻心里涌起一股狂喜——哪怕一线渺茫的成功希望，也能让一个血性的人这样振奋。他听见远处许多人的呐喊，接着又是一声枪响，一颗远距离射来的火枪弹在他和他那些野蛮的敌人之间激起一小柱沙尘。要是敌人等他冲到，他下一步就蹿进他们中间了，可惜他抡起的手臂无物可击。黑色的脊背高高跃起，或贴着草皮平窜，直奔丛林边上而去。

他把手中的棍子掷向最近的一对黑肩膀，猛地站住。高草摇晃着静止下来，一片吼叫和锐利的尖嚎渐渐熄灭于一声凄凉的哀嚎，忽然间，林木葱茏的海岸和蓝色的海湾仿佛坠入一片光亮的寂静的魔咒。这变化像从梦中醒来一样骇人。突如其来的寂静让林加德觉得不可思议。

===Original===

He broke it by lifting his voice in a stentorian shout, which arrested the pursuit of his men. They retired reluctantly, glaring back angrily at the wall of a jungle where not a single leaf stirred. The strangers, whose opportune appearance had decided the issue of that adventure, did not attempt to join in the pursuit but halted in a compact body on the ground lately occupied by the savages.

Lingard and the young leader of the Wajo traders met in the splendid light of noonday, and amidst the attentive silence of their followers, on the very spot where the Malay seaman had lost his life. Lingard, striding up from one side, thrust out his open palm; Hassim responded at once to the frank gesture and they exchanged their first handclasp over the prostrate body, as if fate had already exacted the price of a death for the most ominous of her gifts﻿—the gift of friendship that sometimes contains the whole good or evil of a life.

“I’ll never forget this day,” cried Lingard in a hearty tone; and the other smiled quietly.

Then after a short pause﻿—“Will you burn the village for vengeance?” asked the Malay with a quick glance down at the dead Lascar who, on his face and with stretched arms, seemed to cling desperately to that earth of which he had known so little.

Lingard hesitated.

“No,” he said, at last. “It would do good to no one.”

“True,” said Hassim, gently, “but was this man your debtor﻿—a slave?”

“Slave?” cried Lingard. “This is an English brig. Slave? No. A free man like myself.”

“*Hai.* He is indeed free now,” muttered the Malay with another glance downward. “But who will pay the bereaved for his life?”

“If there is anywhere a woman or child belonging to him, I﻿—my serang would know﻿—I shall seek them out,” cried Lingard, remorsefully.

“You speak like a chief,” said Hassim, “only our great men do not go to battle with naked hands. O you white men! O the valour of you white men!”

“It was folly, pure folly,” protested Lingard, “and this poor fellow has paid for it.”

“He could not avoid his destiny,” murmured the Malay. “It is in my mind my trading is finished now in this place,” he added, cheerfully.

Lingard expressed his regret.

“It is no matter, it is no matter,” assured the other courteously, and after Lingard had given a pressing invitation for Hassim and his two companions of high rank to visit the brig, the two parties separated.

===Chinese===

他放开嗓门一声断喝，打破了寂静，喝住了还在追赶的手下。他们悻悻退回，怒视着那道连一片叶子也不动的丛林之墙。那几个生客，因其及时出现决定了这场冒险的结局，却没有加入追赶，只在野人方才占据的地面上密集地站定。

林加德和瓦久商人的年轻首领，在正午灿亮的阳光里，在双方随从专注的寂静中，在马来水手丧命的那块地上相会了。林加德从一边大步跨上，摊开手掌；哈西姆立刻响应这坦率的姿态，两人隔着那具俯卧的尸体握了第一次手，仿佛命运已经为它最不祥的馈赠——有时包含着一生全部的善与恶的、友谊的馈赠——预先索去了一条性命的代价。

“这一天我永远不会忘记。”林加德衷心地说；对方只静静地笑了笑。

顿了片刻——“你要烧了这村子报仇吗？”马来人问，目光飞快地扫向那个死去的印度水手，他面朝下躺着，双臂张开，仿佛还死死抓住这片他生前所知甚少的土地。

林加德犹豫了。

“不。”他终于说。“烧了它对谁都没好处。”

“说的是。”哈西姆温声道，“可这个人欠你的债吗——是个奴隶？”

“奴隶？”林加德喊道。“这是条英国双桅船。奴隶？不。是跟我一样的自由人。”

“嗨。他现在可真是自由了。”马来人又低头看了一眼，喃喃道。“可谁赔偿遗属的性命钱呢？”

“他要有妻儿老小，不管在哪里，我——我的水手长会知道——我一定找出来。”林加德痛悔地大声说。

“你说话像个头人。”哈西姆说，“只是我们的贵人上阵从不赤手空拳。哦，你们白人！你们白人的胆量啊！”

“那是蠢，纯粹的蠢。”林加德抗议道，“这个可怜人付出了代价。”

“他躲不过他的命。”马来人低声道。“依我看，我在这地方的买卖到此为止了。”他又轻松地补了一句。

林加德表示遗憾。

“不妨事，不妨事。”对方客气地安慰他。林加德又热忱地邀请哈西姆和他的两位高贵同伴上双桅船做客，随后两拨人分了手。

===Original===

The evening was calm when the Malay craft left its berth near the shore and was rowed slowly across the bay to Lingard’s anchorage. The end of a stout line was thrown on board, and that night the white man’s brig and the brown man’s prau swung together to the same anchor.

The sun setting to seaward shot its last rays between the headlands, when the body of the killed Lascar, wrapped up decently in a white sheet, according to Mohammedan usage, was lowered gently below the still waters of the bay upon which his curious glances, only a few hours before, had rested for the first time. At the moment the dead man, released from slip-ropes, disappeared without a ripple before the eyes of his shipmates, the bright flash and the heavy report of the brig’s bow gun were succeeded by the muttering echoes of the encircling shores and by the loud cries of sea birds that, wheeling in clouds, seemed to scream after the departing seaman a wild and eternal goodbye. The master of the brig, making his way aft with hanging head, was followed by low murmurs of pleased surprise from his crew as well as from the strangers who crowded the main deck. In such acts performed simply, from conviction, what may be called the romantic side of the man’s nature came out; that responsive sensitiveness to the shadowy appeals made by life and death, which is the groundwork of a chivalrous character.

Lingard entertained his three visitors far into the night. A sheep from the brig’s sea stock was given to the men of the prau, while in the cabin, Hassim and his two friends, sitting in a row on the stern settee, looked very splendid with costly metals and flawed jewels. The talk conducted with hearty friendship on Lingard’s part, and on the part of the Malays with the well-bred air of discreet courtesy, which is natural to the better class of that people, touched upon many subjects and, in the end, drifted to politics.

“It is in my mind that you are a powerful man in your own country,” said Hassim, with a circular glance at the cuddy.

===Chinese===

傍晚风平浪静，那条马来帆船离开了岸边的泊位，缓缓划过海湾，来到林加德的锚地。一根粗缆的绳头抛上了大船，那一夜，白人的双桅船和棕人的普拉帆船，同拴一锚，一同摇荡。

夕阳向海沉落，最后的光线射过两岬之间；遇害印度水手的遗体，按穆斯林的规矩用白布裹得整整齐齐，轻轻放入海湾纹丝不动的水中——就在几个小时之前，他好奇的目光才头一次落在这片水上。解开脱缆索、死者毫无涟漪地消失在同伴眼前的这一刻，双桅船艏炮明亮的火光和沉重的轰响之后，四周海岸滚过低低的回声，成群盘旋的海鸟高声啼叫，仿佛在为这远行的水手呐喊着一场粗野而永恒的诀别。双桅船的船长垂着头走向船尾，身后跟来他船员们以及挤在主甲板上的生客们低低的、惊喜的赞叹。在这类出于信念而径直做出来的举动里，便显出了这个人性情中可称为浪漫的一面：那种对生与死的朦胧召唤的感应，正是侠义性格的根基。

林加德款待他的三位客人直到深夜。双桅船海上存栏的一只羊送给了普拉帆船上的水手；而在舱里，哈西姆和他的两位朋友并排坐在艉部长榻上，满身的贵金属和有瑕疵的宝石，看去富丽堂皇。谈话进行得热忱友好——林加德这边如此，马来人那边则带着那个民族上等阶层天生得体的、有分寸的客气——谈了许多话题，末了转到了政事上。

“依我看，你在你自己的国家里是个有势力的人。”哈西姆说着，朝餐舱环视了一圈。

===Original===

“My country is upon a faraway sea where the light breezes are as strong as the winds of the rainy weather here,” said Lingard; and there were low exclamations of wonder. “I left it very young, and I don’t know about my power there where great men alone are as numerous as the poor people in all your islands, Tuan Hassim. But here,” he continued, “here, which is also my country﻿—being an English craft and worthy of it, too﻿—I am powerful enough. In fact, I am Rajah here. This bit of my country is all my own.”

The visitors were impressed, exchanged meaning glances, nodded at each other.

“Good, good,” said Hassim at last, with a smile. “You carry your country and your power with you over the sea. A Rajah upon the sea. Good!”

Lingard laughed thunderously while the others looked amused.

“Your country is very powerful﻿—we know,” began again Hassim after a pause, “but is it stronger than the country of the Dutch who steal our land?”

“Stronger?” cried Lingard. He opened a broad palm. “Stronger? We could take them in our hand like this﻿—” and he closed his fingers triumphantly.

“And do you make them pay tribute for their land?” enquired Hassim with eagerness.

“No,” answered Lingard in a sobered tone; “this, Tuan Hassim, you see, is not the custom of white men. We could, of course﻿—but it is not the custom.”

“Is it not?” said the other with a sceptical smile. “They are stronger than we are and they want tribute from us. And sometimes they get it﻿—even from Wajo where every man is free and wears a kris.”

There was a period of dead silence while Lingard looked thoughtful and the Malays gazed stonily at nothing.

“But we burn our powder amongst ourselves,” went on Hassim, gently, “and blunt our weapons upon one another.”

He sighed, paused, and then changing to an easy tone began to urge Lingard to visit Wajo “for trade and to see friends,” he said, laying his hand on his breast and inclining his body slightly.

“Aye. To trade with friends,” cried Lingard with a laugh, “for such a ship”﻿—he waved his arm﻿—“for such a vessel as this is like a household where there are many behind the curtain. It is as costly as a wife and children.”

The guests rose and took their leave.

“You fired three shots for me, Panglima Hassim,” said Lingard, seriously, “and I have had three barrels of powder put on board your prau; one for each shot. But we are not quits.”

===Chinese===

“我的国家在遥远的海上，那里的微风，跟这里雨季的狂风一样有力。”林加德说；四下响起低低的惊叹。“我很年轻就离开了它，我不知道我在那边有什么势力——那边光是大人物，就比你所有岛上的穷人加起来还多，哈西姆老爷（Tuan Hassim）。可是在这里，”他继续说，“这里也是我的国家——这是一条英国船，也配得上是——我的势力就够大了。说实在的，我在这里是拉者。我的国家的这一小块，整个儿是我自己的。”

客人们被打动了，彼此交换着意味深长的眼色，互相点头。

“好，好。”哈西姆终于笑着说。“你把你的国家和你的势力装在船上漂洋过海。海上拉者。好！”

林加德大笑如雷，其余的人也面露愉悦。

“你们的国家很强大——我们知道。”哈西姆顿了顿又开口，“可它比那些窃据我们土地的荷兰人的国家更强吗？”

“更强？”林加德喊道。他摊开一只宽大的手掌。“更强？我们能这样把他们攥在手心里——”说着得意地把手指握拢了。
